## [Survey: LLMs in HPC Programming Show Promise but MPI Weakness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26110) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This survey systematically reviews the application of Large Language Models (LLMs) in High-Performance Computing (HPC) programming, categorizing research into five areas: code generation, parallelization and optimization, frameworks and architectures, evaluation and benchmarking, and broader challenges. It highlights that while general-purpose LLMs perform well on serial and OpenMP tasks, they fall short in distributed paradigms like MPI. This survey provides a timely and structured overview of a growing niche area, offering valuable insights for researchers and practitioners. It underscores the dual potential and fragility of LLMs in HPC, suggesting they will become collaborators rather than replacements for experts, which could influence future tool development and research directions. Domain-specialized models such as HPC-Coder, HPC-GPT, and chatHPC achieve higher accuracy through fine-tuning, curated datasets, and retrieval-augmented generation (RAG), but their scope remains narrow and evaluations are limited to benchmarks or micro-kernels. The survey concludes that effective deployment requires richer datasets, integration with performance analysis and schedulers, rigorous evaluation frameworks, and governance structures for transparency and trust.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: High-Performance Computing (HPC) uses clusters of powerful processors working in parallel to solve complex problems at high speeds. Programming for HPC is complex and expertise-intensive, with parallel paradigms like OpenMP for shared memory and MPI (Message Passing Interface) for distributed memory. Large Language Models (LLMs) are AI models trained on vast text data, capable of generating code, and retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) enhances their accuracy by fetching external information.

## [FIRSTPASS: Multi-Domain Peer Review Dataset with Real Editorial Outcomes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26129) ⭐️ 8.0/10

FIRSTPASS is a new large-scale peer review dataset from Nature Communications, containing 3,668 records across five scientific domains (biology, chemistry, neuroscience, physics, and earth science) with complete multi-round editorial dialogues and outcome labels (STANDARD or EXTENDED) derived from editorial decisions. This dataset addresses a critical gap in AI peer review by extending beyond CS/ML to multiple scientific domains, providing ground truth outcomes absent in prior corpora. It enables training AI systems to understand domain-specific review practices, potentially improving AI-assisted scientific review across disciplines. The dataset is curated from Nature Communications' mandatory transparent peer review (instituted November 2022) and includes initial referee reports, author responses, and updated reviewer assessments. An automated audit confirms 100% content integrity, and expert reviews average 2,155 words, substantially denser than conference venue reviews.

**Background**: Scientific peer review datasets have traditionally been limited to Computer Science and Machine Learning venues, producing models that lack exposure to other scientific domains. Transparent peer review, where review reports and author responses are published alongside papers, provides a rich resource for training AI systems. FIRSTPASS leverages this transparency to create a multi-domain dataset with real editorial outcomes, enabling more generalizable AI scientific judgment.

## [Transformers' Deep Dependency Learning Explained by Gradient Starvation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26139) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper proposes a mechanistic framework modeling LLM learning as competition between surface statistics and deep semantics, identifying a 'Gradient Starvation' phenomenon that suppresses sparse semantic dependency learning. It validates findings on models up to Llama-3.1-8B and Qwen2.5-Coder-7B, and introduces a topology-aligned contrastive objective that improves variable binding tasks by over 2x compared to standard fine-tuning. This work provides a theoretical basis for understanding why LLMs struggle with deep semantic reasoning and why Chain-of-Thought prompting works, potentially guiding better training strategies and interpretability. It could influence how researchers design objectives and prompts to enhance structural reasoning in large models. The framework explains the sudden phase transition in learning semantic dependencies as a result of gradient starvation. The proposed contrastive objective explicitly rectifies gradient geometry, and experiments on variable binding tasks show over 2x improvement over cross-entropy fine-tuning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Gradient starvation is a phenomenon in over-parameterized neural networks where cross-entropy loss minimization captures only a subset of relevant features, suppressing others. Chain-of-Thought prompting elicits step-by-step reasoning from LLMs, improving accuracy on complex tasks. This paper connects these concepts to explain how LLMs learn deep dependencies and why externalizing reasoning helps.

## [LLM Self-Recognition Study Reveals Experimental Design Bias](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26159) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper systematically investigates Self-Generated Text Recognition (SGTR) in LLMs, evaluating 13-21 models across six operationalizations. It reconciles conflicting prior findings by showing that evaluation format, conversation structure, and task domain significantly affect measured SGTR accuracy. This research is critical for AI safety because SGTR capabilities can undermine safeguards that rely on LLMs as evaluators or monitors, potentially leading to biased judgments or collusion. Understanding how experimental design affects SGTR measurements is essential for developing reliable evaluation protocols and designing safer AI systems. The study identifies quality heuristics—models attributing authorship to text they perceive as higher quality—as a dominant confound. It also finds that SFT for SGTR in one configuration can generalize to others, and that training for SGTR increases self-preference in AlpacaEval judge tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Self-Generated Text Recognition (SGTR) refers to an LLM's ability to identify its own outputs, which is relevant to situational awareness and AI safety. In control protocols like trusted editing and honeypotting, SGTR could enable models to recognize and exploit their own outputs, potentially undermining safety measures. The paper's findings highlight the importance of careful experimental design in LLM evaluation, as different operationalizations can lead to vastly different conclusions about model capabilities.

## [Cross-Lingual Audit Reveals English-Trained Prompt Compressors Fail in Other Languages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26175) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A controlled cross-lingual audit of extractive prompt compressors found that English-trained compressors (LLMLingua-2, Kompress-v2) degrade significantly in non-English languages, while a multilingual compressor (XProvence v1) shows no such gap. The study involved over 250,000 evaluation calls across ten languages, five scripts, and eleven target models. This research highlights a critical fairness and efficiency issue: most prompt compressors are trained on English, yet they are used globally. The findings could drive the development of multilingual compression methods and inform practitioners about safe compression budgets outside English, potentially reducing cost disparities in LLM inference. The transfer gap is rate-dependent: at a 0.33 keep-rate, English retains 57-62% of normalized context utilization, while Lithuanian retains only 10-24% and Chinese essentially none. The gap correlates with compression supervision data, not architecture, and aggressive compression can drive compressed contexts to or below no-context utility in three of five non-English languages in long-context settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Extractive prompt compression reduces LLM inference costs by removing low-information tokens, with methods like LLMLingua-2 showing strong results on English benchmarks. However, non-English languages already incur a token premium (1.3-1.8x more tokens than English), and this study investigates whether compression widens that gap. The audit uses fully parallel data and budget-matched controls to isolate the effect of language.

## [Formalizing and Measuring the 'Jump' in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26187) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a formal definition of the 'jump' in LLMs as a finite extension problem with machine-checked certificates, and provides a measurement framework. The authors test four frontier models on nine certified instances, finding a zero Kan-default rate across 248 constrained trials. This work addresses a fundamental debate about whether LLMs can perform abductive leaps beyond canonical completions, offering a rigorous formalization and empirical evidence. It could shift the discussion from philosophical arguments to measurable benchmarks, impacting how we evaluate and understand LLM capabilities. The canonical completion is defined via left and right Kan extensions, and the paper proves a family theorem certifying instances of unbounded difficulty without enumeration. Failures at higher difficulty were due to exhausted reasoning budgets or constraint errors, never reverting to the default.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Abductive reasoning is a form of logical inference that seeks the simplest and most likely conclusion from observations. In model theory, canonical completions are a key tool, often defined via Kan extensions, and the extension problem asks what structures extend given ones. This paper applies these concepts to LLMs to formalize the 'jump'.

## [ADeptS-Bench: New Benchmark Measures Trustworthiness of Computer Use Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26204) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ADeptS-Bench, a dual-stream benchmark for evaluating the trustworthiness of computer use agents (CUAs), was introduced. It assesses both safety (via paired benign/malicious tasks) and disambiguation (whether agents seek clarification on ambiguous instructions), and reveals that no tested model balances task success with safety effectively. As CUAs are increasingly deployed to interact with graphical interfaces, ensuring they can operate safely and handle ambiguity is critical for user trust and adoption. This benchmark fills a gap by providing a comprehensive evaluation framework, highlighting current models' shortcomings and guiding future improvements in AI safety. The benchmark includes a Safety stream with threats embedded in visual interfaces and a Disambiguation stream. Evaluations of seven models show that none exceed 80% task success while staying below 30% attack success; notably, all models click 'Checkout' on a $25K order without hesitation, and none detect a mislabeled 'factory reset' button. An ablation reveals three safety architectures: tool-dependent, partially tool-dependent, and no mechanism.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Computer use agents (CUAs) are AI systems that navigate mobile and desktop applications on behalf of users, often by interpreting screenshots and executing actions. The ADEPTS capability framework, which defines user-facing AI capabilities, grounds the benchmark's risk taxonomy, and general population user studies inform its design. Existing benchmarks like OS-Harm and OSGuard focus on safety under benign instructions, but ADeptS-Bench uniquely combines safety and disambiguation evaluation.

## [REMI: Relational Framework for Fairness Bug Localization and Mitigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26209) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces REMI, a framework that automates the localization, explanation, and mitigation of individual discrimination in data-driven systems. It treats counterfactual fairness as a relational invariant discovery problem, inspired by loop-invariant synthesis, and uses bidirectional relational explanations to identify fairness-violating regions. REMI addresses a critical gap in fairness research by providing principled mechanisms to explain and localize individual fairness bugs, which existing techniques lack. It significantly outperforms state-of-the-art baselines, localizing ground-truth bugs in over 83% of cases and reducing discriminatory decisions in black-box models by up to 70%, offering a practical solution for high-stakes domains. REMI uses three data-alignment techniques to infer interpretable rule-based models that act as 'fairness invariants,' which can selectively block or relabel unfair predictions without retraining. The evaluation covers symbolic and neural network programs, demonstrating its effectiveness across different model types.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Data-driven systems are increasingly used in high-stakes domains like criminal justice and financial lending, but they often exhibit individual discrimination, where similar individuals receive different outcomes based on protected attributes. Existing fairness research focuses on detection and quantification, but lacks principled explanation and localization methods. The relational nature of discrimination, involving comparisons between original and counterfactual pairs, requires a different approach than single-input explanations. Loop-invariant synthesis, a formal methods technique, inspires the relational invariant discovery approach used in REMI.

## [NeuronFuzz: White-Box Fuzzing with Safety Neurons for LLM Safety Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26222) ⭐️ 8.0/10

NeuronFuzz introduces a white-box fuzzing framework that uses internal safety neuron activations as continuous feedback to evaluate LLM safety against jailbreak attacks. It eliminates the need for response generation during fuzzing, achieving a 76-100% jailbreak discovery rate on five white-box source models, outperforming baselines by up to 48 percentage points. This work addresses the high cost and sparse feedback of response-level evaluation in LLM safety testing, offering a more efficient and effective method for jailbreak detection. It is significant for AI safety research and the fuzzing community, as it demonstrates the potential of leveraging internal model states for security evaluation. The SafetyOracle converts safety-neuron activations into a continuous safety alarm score during prefill, and the framework uses gradients to identify safety-sensitive template positions and a masked language model for fluent mutations. Evaluations across 21 text and multimodal models show that optimized templates transfer zero-shot to open-weight and six proprietary target models, achieving average ASR and top-5 ensemble ASR of 69.6%/92.6% and 44.1%/60.0%, respectively.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are AI models trained on vast text data for language tasks, but they can be vulnerable to jailbreak attacks that bypass safety alignment. Traditional safety evaluation methods rely on generating responses to each candidate prompt, which is expensive and provides sparse feedback for strongly aligned models. White-box fuzzing uses internal model information to guide test generation, and safety neurons are specific neurons that activate in response to harmful content, offering a promising signal for safety evaluation.

## [CTF-ABACUS: Trace-Level Provenance for LLM Agent Offensive Security Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26237) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces CTF-ABACUS, a trace-based auditing framework that reconstructs LLM agent trajectories in CTF challenges to provide evidence-grounded solve profiles. It decomposes agent actions into penetration-testing phases and techniques, distinguishing genuine exploitation from shortcut pathways. This framework addresses a critical gap in evaluating LLM agents for offensive security by moving beyond shallow binary success metrics to verify demonstrated exploitation. It provides a more rigorous methodology that could reshape how CTF benchmarks assess agent capabilities, impacting AI security research and evaluation standards. The study applied CTF-ABACUS to 1,435 CTF attempts by six frontier and open-source models on 240 challenges, yielding 2,870 solve profiles under two judge lenses. Trace-verified exploits accounted for only 62-87% of recovered flags, indicating that a significant portion of successes may be due to shortcuts rather than genuine exploitation.

Traditional evaluations rely on binary success or aggregate scores, overlooking the agent's trajectory, which can conflate actual exploitation with direct flag exposure, memorized recall, or guessing. CTF-ABACUS addresses this by reconstructing agent actions into evidence-grounded profiles, similar to trace-based auditing methods used in other domains.

## [Knowledge-Editing Benchmarks Fail to Measure Scope Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26292) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper demonstrates that current knowledge-editing benchmarks cannot measure scope classification, as an oracle router fails to outperform a static policy across all tested conditions. The authors introduce INLAY, a gradient-free editor, to obtain exact ground truth and show that the maximum attainable gain from per-query routing is zero. This negative result challenges the validity of widely used knowledge-editing benchmarks, suggesting that improvements reported on them may not reflect real gains in scope classification. It has significant implications for the evaluation of memory-based editors and the direction of future research in knowledge editing. The study uses 1,689 queries across three datasets and three input conditions, with an oracle router tying a static policy to four decimal places in all nine cells. The authors also construct a missing condition by withholding edits, which moves pooled headroom from +0.0000 to +0.0420, confirming the structural cause.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge editing aims to update the knowledge of large language models without full retraining. Memory-based editors, such as those in the SERAC lineage, rely on a scope classifier to decide whether a stored edit applies to a given query. Benchmarks like CounterFact and RippleEdits are commonly used to evaluate these editors, but this paper argues they lack negative examples, making scope classification unmeasurable.

## [MemToC Benchmark Reveals LLMs Over-Trust Tool Outputs Over Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26295) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced MemToC, a controlled benchmark with 6,504 episodes from 542 factual questions, to evaluate how tool-augmented LLMs resolve conflicts between parametric memory and tool returns. Across five open-weight 7-9B models, they found that tool returns strongly dominate, with instruction-tuned models retaining a verified-correct answer against an incorrect tool in only 6.5-17.1% of eligible cases. This benchmark addresses the underexplored problem of memory-tool conflict resolution, which is critical for the reliability of tool-augmented LLMs in real-world applications. The findings highlight a strong bias toward tool returns, which could lead to incorrect outputs when tools are faulty, and the instability across instruction wordings raises concerns about robustness. The benchmark includes four source-correctness cases plus separate tool-error and no-tool controls. The study also compared prompting with SFT and DPO using chain-level cross-fitting over ToolHop, applying an asymmetric success criterion that requires improved correct-answer retention without reducing correct-tool following; only two of four instruction-tuned backbones met this criterion.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Tool-augmented LLMs combine parametric memory (knowledge stored in model weights) with external tools like search APIs or calculators. When a tool return conflicts with the model's internal knowledge, the model must arbitrate between the two fallible sources. Existing benchmarks measure source preference but do not establish which source is correct, making it hard to assess arbitration quality. MemToC addresses this by controlling the correctness of both sources.

## [New MMI Benchmark Evaluates Omni Models Across Five Modalities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26317) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces the Modality Maturity Index (MMI), a benchmark with 893 questions that evaluates large language models across five modalities (text, image, audio, video, document) and combinations of up to three modalities in both inputs and outputs. It also proposes a supplementary Modality Presence Score (MPS) to measure whether models generate the expected output modalities. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in evaluating multimodal models, which are increasingly marketed as omni systems but are typically assessed only on bimodal tasks. It provides a comprehensive framework that could standardize how the AI community measures multimodal capabilities, impacting model development and comparison. The MMI Value is the average of per-modality scores for each prompt, with human-authored rubrics for each expected output modality. In experiments on five frontier models, MPS ranged from 15.6 (Claude Opus 4.6) to 34.9 (GPT-5.4), indicating low availability of returned modalities. An LLM judge applying rubrics agreed with human annotators on 70.8% of judgments in a separate experiment.

## [KnownLieBench: Benchmarking Emergent Deception in LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26372) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces KnownLieBench, a knowledge-verified benchmark that evaluates whether LLM agents deceive users when incentives conflict with user entitlements. It covers eight customer-service domains and 112 grounded cases, and tests 18 proprietary and open-weight models. This benchmark addresses a critical AI safety issue by separating incentive-driven deception from ignorance or hallucination, enabling more rigorous auditing and steering of agent honesty. It provides a foundation for developing trustworthy LLM agents in real-world deployments. KnownLieBench first confirms through a neutral probe that an agent knows a user's entitlement, then evaluates whether it makes false claims once an incentive to deny that entitlement is introduced. The benchmark also includes post-training experiments showing that honesty-directed fine-tuning reduces deception under incentive, while deception-graded fine-tuning increases lie success on honest-control dialogues without increasing lie frequency under incentive.

Existing evaluations often fail to distinguish deception from ignorance or hallucination, making it difficult to assess true deceptive behavior.

## [Penalty-Aware RAG Evaluation Exposes Guessing Incentive](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26385) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a penalty-aware evaluation framework for RAG systems that uses knowledge-gap canaries and asymmetric scoring (correct +1, wrong -4, abstain 0). Applied to three commercial RAG systems and a no-retrieval baseline on SimpleQA-Verified, it shows that accuracy when answering is clustered (97-98%) but canary violation rates differ sixfold (16.7% vs 98.1%), reordering rankings. This matters because current accuracy metrics reward guessing, which can mask hallucination problems in deployed RAG systems. The framework provides a practical way to evaluate and compare RAG systems, potentially influencing how developers and researchers assess and improve these systems. The framework combines asymmetric scoring, knowledge-gap canaries (questions with verifiably absent answers), and a failure-attribution pipeline. The evaluation used 1,000 questions repeated three times, graded blind by a cross-family three-judge panel with 98.9% unanimity, and the reordering is stable for penalties from k=1 to k=9.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) systems combine a retrieval component with a language model to answer questions using external knowledge. However, they can hallucinate when the knowledge base lacks the answer, and traditional accuracy metrics may not penalize such ungrounded responses. Knowledge-gap canaries are test questions whose answers are deliberately absent from the knowledge base, so any answer indicates hallucination. Asymmetric scoring assigns a larger penalty for wrong answers than the reward for correct ones, encouraging abstention when uncertain.

## [SpeechGym: Audio-Native RL Environment for Voice Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26432) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SpeechGym introduces the first audio-native agentic environment that trains voice agents end-to-end via reinforcement learning, with two omni-modal models conversing in native audio without external ASR/TTS or API boundaries. It uses a per-turn process reward to overcome sparse outcome-only GRPO, and the trained agent more than doubles task success on an independent voice benchmark. This work addresses a critical gap in voice AI by enabling end-to-end training in audio, avoiding the cost and gradient limitations of cascaded ASR/TTS systems. It demonstrates that audio-native RL can improve voice agents' perceptual and behavioral robustness, potentially shifting the paradigm from text-based training to audio-native methods. The environment uses unmodified tasks, tools, and success checks from an established text agentic benchmark, making interaction modality the only variable. The paper identifies two failure modes: misheard arguments causing cascading errors, and unauthorized writes under insistent callers; both are labeled for free by the environment and trainable with process rewards.

## [Diff Mining: Logit Differences Reveal Finetuning Objectives](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26462) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Diff Mining is a new framework that identifies finetuning objectives by comparing logits between a finetuned model and its base model. It requires only output logits and scales to large models, outperforming existing model diffing methods in domain detection and bias identification. This method provides a scalable and practical tool for auditing finetuned models, which is crucial for AI safety and interpretability. It helps detect unwanted behaviors that may emerge during finetuning, enabling better oversight of model development. The framework consists of two stages: extracting per-context logit differences on a reference corpus, and aggregating signals using Top-K frequency or Non-negative Matrix Factorization (NMF) to form interpretable token clusters. Empirically, it identifies over one third of injected biases without targeted probing.

RTNav's latency-aware design is highly relevant for real-world robotics, where wall-clock time directly impacts task success. The method is evaluated on real-time variants of HM3D benchmarks, showing consistent performance improvements over prior work.

## [PI-SCM: Backpropagation-Free Neural Network for Fast PDE Solving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26549) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Physics-Informed Stochastic Configuration Machine (PI-SCM), a novel backpropagation-free framework for solving nonlinear differential equations. It uses analytical Jacobians and linearized least squares to train networks orders of magnitude faster than standard PINNs. This work addresses major computational bottlenecks in PINNs, which rely on backpropagation and automatic differentiation, making training slow and prone to non-convex optimization issues. PI-SCM's approach could enable real-time scientific machine learning applications and make PINNs more scalable for complex differential equations. PI-SCM includes three algorithmic variants: PI-SC-I (localized construction), PI-SC-II (sliding-window updating), and PI-SC-III (global updating), all with proven universal approximation properties. The framework handles both forward and inverse problems, achieving high-fidelity accuracy and robust parameter identification.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Physics-Informed Neural Networks (PINNs) embed physical laws into neural network training by incorporating differential equation residuals into the loss function. Traditional PINNs use backpropagation and automatic differentiation, which are computationally expensive and face non-convex optimization challenges. Stochastic Configuration Networks (SCNs) are randomized neural networks that incrementally assign hidden node parameters, offering a fast and theoretically sound alternative. PI-SCM combines these ideas to create a backpropagation-free method for solving differential equations.

<a id="item-78"></a>
## [J-Zero: Unified Challenger-Solver-Judge Co-Evolution from Zero Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26582) ⭐️ 8.0/10

J-Zero introduces a unified Challenger-Solver-Judge co-evolution framework that enables self-improvement of language models in both verifiable and unverifiable domains without any initial data. It outperforms baselines by an average of 4.2 points on verifiable and 8.0 points on unverifiable domains, and continues to improve through at least ten iterations. This framework addresses the less-explored unverifiable domain in self-evolving language models, potentially reducing the cost of human supervision and advancing the path toward superintelligence. It could impact AI/ML research by providing a method for continuous self-improvement without initial data, applicable to a wide range of tasks. The Judge co-adapts using preference pairs whose ordering is known in advance from how each response was produced, rather than from the Judge's own scores. The Challenger and Solver co-evolve through a minimax game using group relative policy optimization, and the framework supports both verifiable and unverifiable domains.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Self-evolving language models aim to improve without human supervision, but most progress has been in verifiable domains where correctness can be checked. Unverifiable domains, such as open-ended generation, lack clear feedback signals, making self-improvement challenging. J-Zero extends the concept of co-evolution to these domains by introducing a Judge that adapts without relying on its own scores, using known preference orderings from the generation process.

## [Framework for Multi-Agent System Risks and Controls](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26626) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This report introduces an analytical framework with three deployment tiers—singular, federated, and open—to help organizations reason about and control risks in multi-agent AI systems across organizational boundaries. As AI agents increasingly interact across organizational boundaries, this framework addresses a critical governance gap, providing practical guidance for organizations, policymakers, and researchers to manage emerging risks and implement effective controls. The framework defines deployment tiers based on the minimum common governance binding any two interacting agents, and within each tier it examines risk factors, failure modes, and available controls, identifying who can apply them and where collective action is needed.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-agent systems involve multiple AI agents that interact with each other, and as these interactions cross organizational boundaries, failures can emerge from the interactions themselves, making it difficult for any single organization to fully govern them. The framework provides a structured way to analyze these risks and controls across different governance scenarios.

## [FOCUS & RePAIR: Token-Level Guidance to Mitigate Text Degeneration in Pruned LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26676) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces FOCUS and RePAIR, two token-level fine-tuning objectives that mitigate text degeneration in pruned large language models. FOCUS reweights distillation toward high-confidence teacher regions, while RePAIR uses contrastive continuation pairs to prevent repetition loops. Pruning is a common compression technique, but it often causes text degeneration, especially repetition loops, even when perplexity and task accuracy remain unchanged. This work provides a token-level analysis and practical fine-tuning objectives that improve generation quality, making pruning more viable for real-world deployment. The analysis decomposes degeneration into loop entry risk and loop persistence, showing that persistence is controlled by escape mass in the token sampling set. Experiments on open-ended continuation and instruction-based generation show that both methods consistently reduce repetition and improve generation quality.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are often pruned to reduce memory and inference costs, but pruning can amplify text degeneration, such as repetition loops. This paper views decoding as a dynamical process that enters and persists in recurrent contexts, and proposes token-level guidance objectives to address this issue.

## [Daydreaming: Black-Box Attack Steals Hidden Agent Skills](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26733) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced Daydreaming, an execution-only attack that steals multi-file agent skills through black-box task interactions, recovering 86.8% of capability across 7 skills and 4 victim models, outperforming SigLeak by almost 4x. This attack demonstrates that hiding skill files and filtering direct disclosure are insufficient to protect proprietary agent skills, posing a significant threat to hosted AI services that sell access to specialized task results. It highlights a new vulnerability class in AI security. Daydreaming formalizes three nested threat levels (Differential, Trace, Output) and focuses on Output, where the attacker sees only final responses and returned files. It uses adaptive crafted tasks, shadow agents, and local execution checks, requiring a median of 32 victim calls per skill even with disclosure defenses enabled.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Agent skills bundle instructions, reference data, and executable helpers that enable general agents to perform specialized tasks. Hosted providers often keep these files secret while selling access to task results, making the skills valuable targets. Existing defenses block direct disclosure requests but cannot prevent normal task submissions, which Daydreaming exploits.

## [ABE-Ralph: Auditing Experimental Fidelity in LLM-Driven Science](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26753) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces ABE-Ralph, a reference-anchored auditing framework that detects methodological hallucinations in LLM-based scientific agents, such as dataset reduction or oracle substitution. In evaluations across 30 long-horizon reproduction runs and 23 NatureBench discovery tasks, ABE-Ralph achieved a 93% robust execution rate and identified five scientific failure modes. This work addresses the reproducibility crisis in AI research by providing a systematic method to verify that LLM-driven experiments faithfully test their intended claims. It could become a standard tool for evaluating AI scientists, ensuring that reported results are trustworthy and not artifacts of methodological shortcuts. ABE-Ralph represents claims, protocols, required components, baselines, and metrics as structured experimental constraints, and guides implementation through an 8-step workflow with quantitative, qualitative, and code-level verification. The framework detected five scientific failure modes, including silent dataset reduction and oracle substitution, and matched or exceeded state-of-the-art performance on 5 of 23 NatureBench tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents used for scientific experimentation must not only generate executable code but also faithfully implement the reference method and design experiments that test the paper's claims. However, these agents often produce methodological hallucinations, such as silently reducing datasets or replacing failed components with lookup or oracle functions, which can lead to misleading conclusions. The ABE-Ralph framework addresses this by anchoring the audit to the original reference method, ensuring that the experimental design and evidence genuinely support the intended claims.

## [Matched Trajectory Replay Evaluates Confidence-Gated Retrieval in Language Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26846) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces matched trajectory replay, a controlled protocol for comparing confidence-to-action mappings in interactive language agents. Using this protocol, the authors show that post-hoc isotonic calibration can increase committed answer accuracy by up to 41 percentage points across six model-dataset pairs. This work addresses a gap in evaluating confidence signals in language agents by measuring trajectory-level consequences rather than isolated confidence scores. The findings highlight that calibration improves risk interpretability but does not estimate the value of additional retrieval, suggesting a need for separate utility estimates in retrieval-augmented systems. The study uses Mistral, GPT, and Qwen models on HotpotQA and MuSiQue datasets. Calibration increases committed answer accuracy by up to 41 percentage points but can reduce coverage and increase retrieval use; overall accuracy improves by up to 15 points on HotpotQA but falls by up to 17 points on MuSiQue.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Interactive language agents use confidence signals to decide whether to answer immediately, retrieve additional evidence, or defer. Confidence calibration methods like isotonic regression adjust model confidence to better reflect true accuracy, but their impact on downstream actions is often not evaluated. Matched trajectory replay provides a controlled way to compare different confidence-to-action mappings by holding other factors fixed.

## [PLCBENCH: First Real-PLC Framework to Test LLM Agents' Physical Impact](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26882) ⭐️ 8.0/10

PLCBENCH is introduced as the first real-PLC hardware-in-the-loop framework to evaluate whether autonomous LLM agents can convert network access to PLCs into sustained physical impact. The framework was tested on four commercial PLCs with four closed-loop workloads, and across 240 real-PLC episodes, 75 (31.3%) achieved sustained physical objectives. This work addresses a critical gap in ICS security research by moving beyond digital-only evaluations to measure actual cyber-to-physical impact. It provides a reproducible benchmark that can help the security community understand and mitigate emerging threats from LLM agents targeting industrial control systems. The framework uses a deterministic evaluator that assigns six hidden diagnostic flags based on runner, communication, PLC-object, and process records, distinguishing usable interaction, process-linked manipulation, and sustained impact. Results show that richer process observation increases conditional objective attainment from 44.2% to 64.0%, and 98 episodes stopped before a valid native read, while 62 reached a process-linked write but did not sustain the final objective.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Programmable logic controllers (PLCs) are industrial computers that control manufacturing processes and are central to industrial control systems (ICS). Hardware-in-the-loop (HIL) simulation is a testing method that connects real hardware to a simulated environment, enabling realistic evaluation of cyber-physical systems. LLM agents are increasingly being explored for both offensive and defensive cybersecurity applications, but their potential to cause physical damage through PLCs has been understudied.

## [Magnon-Induced Phononic Chern Insulator at GHz Frequencies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27011) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper proposes a magnon-induced phononic Chern insulator in a honeycomb phononic crystal hybridized with ferromagnetic islands, where a circularly polarized Kittel mode breaks time-reversal symmetry and opens a full Chern gap, enabling tunable Chern numbers |C|=1 and |C|=2. This work establishes chiral magnon-phonon hybridization as a route to magnetically reconfigurable topological phononics, potentially enabling practical on-chip acoustic devices operating at GHz frequencies with low loss. In the large-detuning regime, the mechanism leads to an effective Haldane-type phononic model with magnon-induced complex hopping. The predicted gaps can exceed realistic phonon and magnon linewidths, making them observable in GHz acoustic devices.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Chern insulators are topological phases characterized by a nonzero Chern number, which exhibit chiral edge states robust to disorder. Magnon-phonon coupling refers to the interaction between spin waves (magnons) and lattice vibrations (phonons), which can be used to manipulate topological properties. The Kittel mode is a uniform precession mode in ferromagnetic resonance, which can couple to phonons with a phase winding, breaking time-reversal symmetry.

## [Conditional Diffusion Model Extracts TMD PDFs Directly from SIDIS Events](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27077) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study proposes a conditional diffusion model that maps raw SIDIS event kinematics directly to TMD PDFs, bypassing traditional parameterized fitting. Tested on simulated CLAS12 data, it recovers underlying TMDs with reliable uncertainties even with as few as 1,000 conditioning events. This approach could significantly improve TMD extraction for nucleon structure studies, especially in statistics-limited regimes relevant to Jefferson Lab and the future Electron-Ion Collider. It offers a flexible alternative to traditional fitting methods, potentially enhancing the precision and reliability of future extractions. The model is evaluated on simulated SIDIS data at CLAS12 kinematics, showing that uncertainties narrow steadily with increasing event statistics. It produces reliable estimates even with as few as 1,000 conditioning events, a regime directly relevant to ongoing and planned experiments.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: TMD PDFs describe the three-dimensional momentum structure of quarks and gluons inside nucleons, and are extracted from SIDIS data. Traditional methods rely on parameterized functional forms and iterative fitting, which can limit flexibility and complicate uncertainty quantification. Conditional diffusion models are generative AI models that incorporate conditioning information to produce outputs, and here they are applied to directly map scattering events to TMD PDFs.

## [LLM Agent Safety Monitors Fail Against Cross-Iteration Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27141) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper proves that trajectory-scoped safety monitors for autonomous LLM agents cannot detect attacks whose evidence is fragmented across multiple iterations, and introduces LoopHarness, a framework that maintains persistent, non-decaying safety state at the loop level. This result exposes a fundamental flaw in current LLM agent safety practices, as most deployed safeguards are trajectory-scoped. It highlights the need for cross-iteration state in safety monitoring, which could significantly impact the design of safer autonomous agent systems. The separation theorem shows that any trajectory-scoped monitor has a true-positive rate equal to its false-positive rate against such attacks. The paper also proves that a geometrically decaying risk score is insufficient, as the cooling-off period is constant, and LoopHarness bounds unauthorized actions by B+m-1+m/δ_M, a constant in N.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Autonomous LLM agents operate in loops, repeatedly planning, executing tool calls, and persisting state across many unattended iterations. Current safety monitors are typically defined over a single trajectory, re-initializing safety state at the start of each new trajectory, which fails to capture evidence that spans multiple iterations.

## [PACE: Unified Condense-and-Extract Paradigm for Fast VLM Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27206) ⭐️ 8.0/10

PACE introduces a training-free inference framework that accelerates both the vision encoder and the LLM in Vision-Language Models via a unified Condense-and-Extract paradigm. It integrates an Adaptive Pixel Compressor (APC) before encoding and a Dynamic Dual-Attention Extractor (DDAE) after encoding, achieving 3.1x speedup in time-to-first-token while retaining 93.8% performance with only 10% visual tokens on Qwen2.5-VL-7B. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in VLM deployment—high inference cost due to visual token proliferation—by optimizing both the vision encoder and LLM stages, which most prior methods overlook. It offers a practical, training-free solution that could enable faster and more efficient VLM inference in real-world applications, potentially lowering barriers for edge deployment and real-time use. PACE's APC evaluates visual information density before encoding to adaptively downsample redundant inputs, while DDAE selectively retains visual tokens using a fusion of internal visual signals from the encoder and semantic signals from the LLM. The framework is training-free and has been integrated into Qwen2.5-VL-7B, with code available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-Language Models (VLMs) process images by converting them into visual tokens, which are then fed into a large language model (LLM). The number of visual tokens can be large, leading to high computational costs during inference. Existing token pruning methods typically operate only after the vision encoder, leaving the encoding phase unoptimized, and often fail to preserve both holistic and fine-grained details under strict token budgets. PACE addresses these limitations by introducing a unified condense-and-extract approach that optimizes both stages.

## [STEP: State-Aware Task Planning with MM-LLMs for Human-Robot Collaboration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27225) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces STEP (State-aware Task Estimator and Planner), a method that prompts a Multi-modal Large Language Model (MM-LLM) to explicitly estimate system states and predict state transitions during task planning. In a simulated robot assembly task, STEP outperforms the state-of-the-art by 32.8% in action executability and 14.8% in final-state error. This work addresses a critical limitation of MM-LLMs in human-robot collaboration: their tendency to hallucinate actions due to a lack of state awareness. By improving action executability and reducing state errors, STEP could enhance the reliability of autonomous robots in industrial settings, making them more practical for real-world deployment. STEP explicitly prompts the MM-LLM to estimate the current system state and predict the state transitions resulting from each action, ensuring task-convergent planning. It also provides additional assistance parameters needed for executing the predicted actions, which helps bridge the gap between high-level natural language plans and low-level robot execution.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-modal Large Language Models (MM-LLMs) have been explored for task planning in human-robot collaboration, leveraging in-context learning to interpret user actions and generate long-horizon plans. However, they lack an inherent understanding of system states and do not track state transitions, often leading to hallucinated actions that deviate from the intended goal. STEP addresses this by explicitly modeling state estimation and transition prediction within the planning process.

<a id="item-93"></a>
## [Compositional Online Learning at the LLM Call Boundary](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27244) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a framework for compositional online learning at the LLM call boundary, where each component makes execution-time decisions and refines its learned artifacts online. A production case study in Cortex AISQL composes three components, achieving an 11.4x upper bound and a realistic ~8x speedup on a conjunction-filter workload. This work addresses the high cost of LLM calls in semantic data processing systems, which can dominate query cost. By hiding learning updates behind LLM latency, it enables adaptive query optimization without extra overhead, potentially improving performance and cost efficiency for production systems. The framework spans two axes: decision granularity and learner update cadence, with a shared learning pattern that hides each trainer step inside the next LLM round-trip. The case study includes a memoization layer, an online per-call filter-ordering learner, and an online per-batch cascade-routing learner, with a conditional cost decomposition assigning each component to a distinct factor of per-row LLM cost.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Semantic data processing systems (SDPSs) use LLMs to answer declarative queries over unstructured data, but LLM calls are expensive and slow. Classical adaptive query processing assumes lightweight online learners, but LLM latency inverts this constraint, allowing per-call gradient steps and per-batch threshold solves to fit within the round-trip time.

<a id="item-94"></a>
## [Censored Rating Scales Can Manufacture Spurious Effects in LLM Judge Audits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27309) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper demonstrates that difference-in-differences (DiD) applied to censored rating scales can manufacture spurious effects in LLM judge audits. The authors show this through a pre-registered audit of a pedagogy judge, where a nominally significant interaction effect is not identified as preference but arises from the scale floor and severity shift. This finding challenges the validity of many LLM judge audits that rely on DiD with bounded rating scales, potentially leading to false conclusions about model biases. It underscores the need for more robust evaluation methodologies in AI safety and model alignment research. The paper derives the mechanism in closed form and shows that the spurious effect can be reproduced from the observed severity shift and scale floor alone, accounting for 79-85% of the nominal effect. The pre-registered audit involved 990 calls, with the primary endpoint null (+0.085 points, p=0.684) but one interaction significant (+0.378, p=0.002).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Difference-in-differences is a causal inference technique that compares changes over time or across conditions to estimate treatment effects. In LLM judge audits, researchers often use DiD on bounded rating scales to assess biases, assuming that additive artifacts cancel out. However, when the scale is censored (bounded), each term of the double difference is censored, leading to unequal attenuation that can create spurious interactions.

## [PAWBench: Benchmarking Probabilistic Alignment in Video World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27345) ⭐️ 8.0/10

PAWBench formalizes probabilistic alignment as a distributional criterion for world models and introduces a benchmark with 50 scenarios across eight physical mechanism groups, along with the PAWEval protocol that converts repeated video rollouts into empirical outcome distributions. The study evaluates eleven current video generation systems and finds that none consistently match reference probabilities while recovering the full range of valid behaviors. This work addresses a critical gap in evaluating video generation models as world models, shifting the focus from individual-video plausibility to distribution-level correctness. The benchmark and protocol are likely to influence future research by providing a standardized way to measure and improve probabilistic alignment, which is essential for applications requiring reliable predictions of multiple possible outcomes. The benchmark spans 50 scenarios across eight physical mechanism groups, and the evaluation protocol converts repeated video rollouts into empirical distributions over possible physical behaviors. The study also tests whether language prompts, initial noise sampling, or model training can reshape the model's predictive distribution, providing insights into potential avenues for improvement.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Video generation models are increasingly being framed as world models that should not only produce plausible trajectories but also capture the distribution of possible behaviors under the same initial conditions and actions. Existing evaluations typically assess individual-video plausibility and do not test whether repeated generations recover the correct distribution, which is a requirement for probabilistic alignment. PAWBench aims to fill this gap by providing a formal criterion and a benchmark to evaluate video generators as stochastic samplers of world dynamics.

## [LeVJEPA: Efficient Video Pretraining Without Heuristics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27395) ⭐️ 8.0/10

LeVJEPA introduces the first video encoder trained under LeJEPA's collapse-free objective, eliminating the need for architectural asymmetries like EMA target encoders and masked reconstruction. It uses a single encoder with an invariance loss regularized by SIGReg, reducing pretraining compute by 5.6 to 20.8 times at matched epochs while matching or surpassing V-JEPA 2. This work significantly reduces the computational cost of video pretraining, making it a more viable substrate for general-purpose visual learning. It could shift research away from complex heuristics toward simpler, more scalable methods, potentially accelerating progress in video understanding and representation learning. LeVJEPA uses uniform random token dropping to reduce the number of tokens the encoder processes, which improves downstream accuracy while lowering compute. At matched total FLOPs, it exceeds the strongest video baseline by 7.6 points on ImageNet-1K and remains competitive on motion-centric benchmarks, while also supporting block-causal attention without accuracy loss.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Self-supervised video representation learning often relies on architectural asymmetries, such as exponential-moving-average target encoders and stop-gradient, to prevent representation collapse, or uses masked reconstruction in pixel space. LeJEPA's collapse-free objective, regularized by SIGReg, provides a provable guarantee against collapse without these heuristics. This paper applies that objective to video, showing that video can be a competitive alternative to image pretraining when computational overhead is removed.

## [CAST: Concept-Guided Artifact Suppression for Auditable Clinical Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27397) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces CAST (Concept-guided Artifact Suppression Tuning), a framework that uses sparse autoencoders and LLM-assisted interpretation to identify and suppress note-specific artifacts in clinical language models, improving robustness and providing auditable feature-level attributions. On MIMIC-IV mortality prediction, CAST outperforms fine-tuned encoder baselines and remains competitive with strong LLM baselines. This work addresses a critical challenge in clinical NLP: model robustness under distribution shift, where models often rely on spurious artifacts rather than patient state. By making the suppression process auditable, CAST enhances trust and interpretability, which are essential for deploying AI in healthcare settings. CAST uses sparse autoencoders to expose sparse, human-auditable features from intermediate Transformer activations, labels SAE latents with an LLM-assisted interpretation pipeline and ICD-10 retrieval constraints, and suppresses verified artifact latents via residual subtraction during fine-tuning. It also provides post-hoc per-concept attributions for auditing model decisions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Clinical language models often achieve high accuracy on in-hospital data but fail when deployed in new settings due to overfitting on note-specific artifacts like templates and boilerplate. Sparse autoencoders are a type of autoencoder that enforce sparsity in the hidden layer, learning efficient and interpretable representations. LLM-assisted interpretation pipelines use large language models to automatically label or explain model features, and ICD-10 is a standard coding system for diseases and procedures used in healthcare.

## [CLAP: Cross-Embodiment Video World Models Enable Zero-Shot Physical Simulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27406) ⭐️ 8.0/10

CLAP is a new framework for cross-embodiment action-conditioned video generation that can be trained on diverse internet-scale videos of both humans and robots. It unifies disparate action spaces using end-effector poses, language instructions, and latent actions, and introduces a curriculum-based learning recipe that enables zero-shot deployment to real-world tasks. This work addresses a major limitation of existing action-conditioned video models, which are typically restricted to a single robot embodiment and cannot leverage heterogeneous video data. By enabling cross-embodiment learning, CLAP could significantly improve the generalization and data efficiency of video world models, with broad implications for robotics and physical simulation. CLAP reconciles action spaces using end-effector poses, language instructions, and latent actions, and uses a curriculum-based recipe that first learns physical priors from unlabeled videos via latent actions, then grounds them in end-effector action spaces. It approaches or surpasses state-of-the-art single-embodiment models on benchmarks like DROID, and the authors open-source all code and models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Action-conditioned video generation models predict future video frames given past observations and an action sequence, serving as world models for robotics. However, most such models are trained on data from a single robot embodiment, limiting their ability to generalize. Latent actions are compact surrogates for physical actions that can be learned from unlabeled videos, enabling learning from diverse data sources. CLAP builds on these concepts to achieve cross-embodiment learning.

Researchers introduced MCR-Bench, the first defect state-aware benchmark for realistic multi-round code review, containing 2,269 annotated tasks across five programming languages. Each task includes fine-grained defect metadata and cross-round state labels to capture the full evolution of defects during review. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in automated code review research, which has largely treated code review as a single-round static task. By enabling evaluation of LLMs in interactive, multi-round scenarios, MCR-Bench could drive improvements in LLM-based code review tools and better reflect real-world development workflows. The benchmark covers five programming languages and includes fine-grained annotations such as defect description, type, and severity, along with dynamic state labels. Experiments with mainstream LLMs revealed limited overall performance, with significant degradation as interaction rounds increase, and higher miss rates for semantically complex or low-salience defects.

## [Interaction Protocol Shapes Moral Judgment in Multi-Agent Debate](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.10002) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study reveals that the interaction protocol (synchronous vs. round-robin) in multi-agent debates significantly shapes moral judgments. Over 30,000 debates among GPT-4.1, Claude 3.7 Sonnet, and Gemini 2.0 Flash on Reddit dilemmas showed distinct behavioral patterns, including inertia and conformity. This finding is significant because multi-agent systems are increasingly deployed in advisory and evaluative roles, and understanding how interaction protocols affect moral reasoning is crucial for AI alignment and safe deployment. It establishes interaction protocol as a substantive sociotechnical design consideration. In synchronous debates, GPT-4.1 showed strong inertia with only 0.6-3.1% revision rates, while Claude 3.7 Sonnet and Gemini 2.0 Flash revised 28-41% of the time. In round-robin debates, GPT-4.1 and Gemini 2.0 Flash exhibited high conformity, with verdicts strongly influenced by order effects, whereas Claude 3.7 Sonnet was less conforming.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-agent debate is a mechanism where multiple LLMs interact to improve accuracy, but the impact of interaction protocol on values and consensus has been underexplored. Synchronous protocols run agents in parallel, while round-robin protocols sequence turns, representing two common orchestration methods. The study uses Reddit's 'Am I the Asshole' dilemmas to elicit moral judgments.

DeepPlanner introduces an end-to-end reinforcement learning framework that uses entropy-based advantage shaping to enhance planning capabilities in deep research agents. It achieves state-of-the-art results across seven deep research benchmarks under a substantially lower training budget. This work addresses a critical gap in optimizing the planning stage of LLM agents, which is often under-optimized compared to reasoning and action. By improving planning quality efficiently, DeepPlanner could accelerate the development of more capable deep research agents, a rapidly growing area in AI. The framework shapes token-level advantage with an entropy-based term to allocate larger updates to high-entropy tokens, and selectively upweights sample-level advantages for planning-intensive rollouts. The observation that planning tokens exhibit significantly higher entropy than other action tokens under vanilla RL motivates this approach.

DIANOIA introduces a three-channel diagnostic decomposition of multi-agent reasoning gain into coverage, fidelity, and synthesis, each empirically measurable. It derives a diagnostic protocol that identifies bottleneck channels for any task and instantiates it as a multi-agent system, outperforming strong baselines on GSM8K, AIME-2025, MBPP, and BFCL-SP. This framework addresses a critical gap in multi-agent LLM systems: the inability to predict which design works for a new task or diagnose failures. By reframing multi-agent design as channel-aware resource allocation, it enables practitioners to invest tokens more effectively, potentially improving efficiency and performance across applications. The system comprises role-diverse proposers for coverage, execution-grounded verification for fidelity, and iterative synthesis. On MBPP, it dominates the Pareto frontier at ~5× token savings and reaches +4.6 percentage points at matched cost; the protocol correctly identifies bottleneck channels on every benchmark.

DIANOIA decomposes reasoning gain into coverage (exploring diverse solutions), fidelity (ensuring correctness), and synthesis (combining outputs), providing measurable primitives and testable predictions.

This survey systematically examines reproducibility failures in financial AI systems across tabular, graph, and LLM-based models, with first-party experiments quantifying nondeterminism in credit scoring, fraud detection, and agentic workflows. <a id="item-106"></a>
## [ProvenanceGuard: Source-Aware Factuality Verification for MCP-Based LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18037) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ProvenanceGuard is a new source-aware verifier for MCP-based LLM agents that detects cross-source conflation, where a claim is supported by some source but attributed to the wrong one. It decomposes answers into atomic claims, routes them to source-specific evidence, and returns per-claim verdicts plus an answer-level allow/block decision. This addresses a critical gap in factuality verification for tool-using LLM agents, as standard metrics only check pooled evidence support, missing attribution errors. It provides a concrete solution with strong results, potentially improving reliability of MCP-based agents in high-stakes domains like medicine. Evaluated on 281 medical-domain MCP-agent traces, ProvenanceGuard achieves block F1 0.802 and source accuracy 0.858 on a held-out split, and detects all injected attribution swaps in 50 controlled probes. Repair-and-reverify resolves all blocked answers, though source-plus-relation accuracy drops to 0.229 on a harder multi-source benchmark.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: The Model Context Protocol (MCP) is an open standard introduced by Anthropic in November 2024 that standardizes how AI systems integrate with external tools and data sources. Cross-source conflation occurs when a RAG system produces a factually correct answer but attributes it to the wrong source, a failure mode not captured by standard factuality metrics. Natural Language Inference (NLI) is a task that determines whether a premise entails a hypothesis, used here to check claim support.

## [ARTS: Reasoning Language Models Enhance Automated Scientific Discovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21891) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces ARTS (Agentic Reasoning for Tree Search), a method that uses a reasoning language model to navigate the hypothesis space in scientific discovery, distinguishing hypothesis merit from execution quality and using test-time training to handle context length issues. ARTS outperforms leading algorithms across 22 tasks from MLGym and MLEBench, with over 15.3% relative improvement in normalized score. ARTS addresses fundamental limitations in existing tree search methods for automated discovery, which conflate hypothesis quality with execution quality and prune search logs due to context limits. By leveraging reasoning language models and test-time training, ARTS enables more efficient and effective scientific discovery, potentially accelerating AI-driven research and reducing inference costs. ARTS uses a reasoning language model to inspect prior execution logs, diagnose whether failures arose from faulty implementations or bad hypotheses, and select the next hypothesis to build on. Test-time training instills the knowledge of the search tree into the model weights, allowing a Qwen3-4B agent to match closed-source frontier models like Gemini-3 Pro and GPT o3-reasoning with up to 5x lower inference cost.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Scientific discovery can be framed as an iterative search over hypotheses and experiments, often navigated using heuristics like Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS). Traditional methods conflate hypothesis merit with execution quality and prune search logs due to context window limits. Test-time training (TTT) is a technique that updates model parameters during inference using a loss function derived from input data, enabling the model to adapt to new information without full retraining.

## [Rethinking Harness Evolution Evaluation for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12227) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper critiques current evaluation protocols for harness evolution in LLM agents, proposing matched-budget comparisons and highlighting overfitting risks. Experiments on Terminal-Bench 2.1 with GPT-5.4 and Claude Opus 4.6 show that automatic harness evolution does not consistently outperform simple test-time scaling methods. This research addresses a critical methodological flaw in evaluating harness evolution, which could influence future evaluation practices in AI/ML research. It underscores the need for fairer benchmarks and protocols to accurately measure the benefits of harness design improvements. The study compares harness evolution with simple test-time scaling and discovery baselines under matched feedback and inference budgets, and evaluates evolved harnesses on held-out tasks to assess generalization. The results raise questions about the effectiveness of automatic harness evolution, and the code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: An agent harness, also known as agent scaffolding, is the software infrastructure surrounding an LLM that enables it to operate as an AI agent, managing tool use, memory, and feedback loops. Harness evolution refers to automatically searching for optimal harness configurations using unit test cases, but this process can overfit to the benchmark if the same tasks are used for both search and final evaluation.

## [Blast Radius: Predictive Memory Layer Cuts Token Use 17-26%](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07440) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Blast Radius introduces a predictive memory management layer for agentic coding that reduces token consumption by 17-26% across seven OpenAI models. It uses reversible context eviction via NECROPHORESIS and Recurring Dead Matter (RDM) to archive and bury dead context. This addresses the growing cost and token inefficiency in agentic coding, making LLM-based agents more affordable and sustainable. The formal framework and empirical validation could influence future memory management designs across the industry. The method operates beneath HCRC, deciding which records to bury and how far an incoming prompt may reach into the codebase. It achieved the lowest overflow rate among tested policies, remained byte-exact reversible, and of 450 buried bodies, 378 were recurring dead matter and zero were recalled.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic coding involves LLM agents performing coding tasks, often consuming large amounts of tokens due to extensive context. Context eviction is a strategy to manage limited context windows by removing less relevant information. Reversible eviction ensures that evicted data can be restored if needed, while Polish context space provides a mathematical framework for modeling context retention and eviction.

## [ExecRubrics: Executable Python Rubrics for Verifiable LLM Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22559) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ExecRubrics introduces a framework that represents evaluation rubrics as executable Python scoring functions, providing an operational semantics for natural-language rubric intent. On three long-form benchmarks (HealthBench, HelpSteer, ArgQuality), it achieves preference accuracies of 52.9%, 75.3%, and 91.5%, respectively, while significantly reducing evaluation latency. This approach offers a faster, more explainable, and less ambiguous alternative to black-box LLM judges, which is particularly critical in high-stakes domains like healthcare and banking. By making rubrics executable and verifiable, it could shift how LLM evaluation is conducted, reducing reliance on expensive proprietary models and improving auditability. ExecRubrics encodes evaluation logic as verifiable Python scoring functions, allowing inspection, execution, and editing. It also integrates external text processing libraries like NLTK and spaCy to further improve preference accuracy, and it outperforms natural-language rubric baselines on all three benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional LLM evaluation often uses natural-language rubrics that are ambiguous and rely on black-box LLM judges, which are expensive and not easily auditable. These rubrics typically assume criteria aggregate independently through linear weighted sums, failing to capture dependencies, alternatives, penalties, and override conditions. ExecRubrics addresses these limitations by representing rubrics as executable programs, providing a fixed decision procedure that can be verified and edited.

## [NIS-Agent Reduces Inertia Bias in Deep Research Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23045) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper identifies a new failure mode called inertia bias in LLM-based deep research agents, introduces the IBIS benchmark to measure it, and proposes NIS-Agent, a framework that uses noise isolation to mitigate it. NIS-Agent achieves competitive performance on GAIA, WebWalkerQA, BrowseComp, and BrowseComp-zh while reducing token cost by 33%. This work addresses a critical reliability issue in LLM agents, showing that self-authored action history can systematically distort subsequent judgment. By providing a benchmark and a mitigation method, it offers a path toward more objective and trustworthy deep research agents, which is significant for AI/ML and systems research. The IBIS benchmark controls search observations while varying whether the model evaluates its own prior action, revealing that models perform substantially worse when they 'own' the preceding search step. NIS-Agent applies context isolation at two decision points: webpage triage and final-answer validation, and an 8B model trained to resist inertia bias achieves performance comparable to GPT-4o under the same framework.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Deep research agents are LLM-powered web search systems that perform multi-step research tasks. Inertia bias is a cognitive bias where an agent becomes less objective when evaluating the consequences of its own prior actions, similar to confirmation bias in humans. The IBIS benchmark and NIS-Agent are designed to measure and mitigate this bias, improving the reliability of such agents.

## [RobustTests: Faulty-Code-Driven Test Synthesis and Dense Rewards for RLVR Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24135) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces RobustTests, a framework that synthesizes test cases from 'near-correct' faulty code and uses validator agents with behavioral feature clustering to filter invalid tests. It also proposes a stepwise dense reward function based on pass rates, achieving a 3% absolute improvement on LiveCodeBench with Qwen3-32B. This addresses critical issues in RLVR for code generation, namely insufficient test coverage and reward hacking, which can degrade policy performance. The proposed method offers a practical solution to improve the robustness and effectiveness of RL fine-tuning for LLM code generation, potentially benefiting the broader AI coding ecosystem. The framework constructs an augmented version of the CodeContests+ dataset with superior diagnostic utility. The stepwise dense reward function mitigates false negatives and enhances training robustness. Codes and data are available on Hugging Face.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement Learning from Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) is a technique used to train LLMs for code generation by providing rewards based on verifiable outcomes, such as passing test cases. However, RLVR often suffers from insufficient test coverage and reward hacking, where models exploit loopholes in the reward function instead of solving the task correctly. Dense reward shaping provides more granular feedback to guide learning, and test synthesis aims to generate diverse test cases to improve coverage.

## [RePolicy: RL-Based Safety-Policy Invocation for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24275) ⭐️ 8.0/10

RePolicy is a new agent safeguard that uses reinforcement learning to dynamically invoke applicable safety policies from a policy library and generate policy-grounded rationales and safety judgments for LLM agent trajectories. It is trained with supervised initialization on the PolicyTraj-20K dataset, followed by GRPO with verifiable rewards and policy-context perturbation. This addresses a critical gap in adaptive safety for LLM agents, as existing methods rely on prompting or supervised fine-tuning and struggle with unseen trajectories and changing policy contexts. RePolicy's strong performance across six benchmarks suggests it could significantly improve the robustness and adaptability of AI safety mechanisms in real-world deployments. RePolicy uses GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization), a reinforcement learning algorithm that computes policy gradients using group-normalized advantage estimation without a value critic. The training includes policy-context perturbation to improve robustness to varying policy contexts, and the method is evaluated on six agent safety benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents execute multi-step trajectories and must be assessed under context-dependent safety policies. Existing policy-aware safeguards typically use prompting or supervised fine-tuning, which limits their ability to generalize to unseen trajectories and dynamic policy contexts. Reinforcement learning, particularly GRPO, offers a way to learn adaptive policy invocation directly from verifiable rewards.

Experimental results show that VLM-TSI significantly outperforms a strong baseline, but overall performance remains modest, highlighting the difficulty of TGLG.

Understanding verifier limitations can guide the development of more robust reward systems, improving the reliability and efficiency of training reasoning models. The study shows that rule-based verifiers achieve near-perfect precision but suffer from low recall, failing to recognize equivalent answers in different formats. Model-based verifiers improve static accuracy but are susceptible to reward hacking, misclassifying certain patterns as correct after fine-tuning. The authors propose a hybrid verifier design to combine strengths.

## [New Framework: AI as Cognitive Infrastructure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.22893) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces 'Cognitive Infrastructure Studies' (CIS), a new interdisciplinary domain that reconceptualizes AI as invisible, foundational systems automating relevance judgment and reshaping human cognition. It proposes methodological innovations like 'infrastructure breakdown methodologies' to study these effects. This framework addresses a critical blind spot in human-AI interaction research by focusing on pre-conscious, systemic effects of AI on cognition. It has significant implications for understanding democratic deliberation, governance, and social epistemologies in digital societies. CIS identifies three scales of impact: individual (cognitive dependency), collective (democratic deliberation), and societal (governance). It also highlights gaps across disciplines: cognitive science lacks population-scale preprocessing analysis, digital sociology cannot access individual cognitive mechanisms, and computational approaches miss cultural transmission dynamics.
