## [GRAPHSU: Graph-Guided Unlearning Expands Deletion Scope Beyond Forget Seeds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26743) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces GRAPHSU, a graph-guided controller that constructs a weighted support-route graph to propagate deletion pressure beyond the explicitly identified forget seeds, applying graded forgetting strengths to high-risk neighbors. On TOFU and PISTOL benchmarks with GPT-2 Medium and Llama-3.2-3B-Instruct, it reduces soft leakage by up to 49.5 percentage points over a seed-only baseline. This addresses a critical limitation in selective unlearning: target knowledge often remains recoverable through paraphrases, aliases, or neighboring examples, which existing methods fail to handle. By expanding the deletion scope, GRAPHSU improves privacy and compliance for enterprises that fine-tune models on proprietary data, offering a more practical alternative to full retraining. GRAPHSU constructs a weighted support-route graph, propagates deletion pressure through it, and applies graded forgetting strengths to high-risk neighbors. It was evaluated on TOFU (synthetic author-profile QA) and PISTOL (structural unlearning benchmark) using GPT-2 Medium and Llama-3.2-3B-Instruct, achieving the lowest utility-feasible soft leakage across all deletion settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Selective unlearning aims to remove specific knowledge from a language model while preserving its overall utility, offering a practical alternative to full retraining. However, existing methods treat the explicitly identified forget examples as the complete deletion scope, which is insufficient when target knowledge remains recoverable through paraphrases, aliases, or neighboring training examples. GRAPHSU addresses this by using a graph-based approach to expand the deletion scope.

The paper introduces ColRel, a two-stage method that uses large language models to discover column relationships in data lakes, particularly effective for coded or abbreviated schemas in industrial ERP datasets. It builds column embeddings from metadata and data at ingestion time, and uses business dictionaries to generate natural-language descriptions for difficult cases. This work addresses a practical challenge in data integration and governance: discovering relationships in data lakes with weak metadata, which is common in ERP systems. By leveraging LLMs, ColRel can improve data usability and enable more effective data management, potentially benefiting enterprises that rely on complex ERP datasets. ColRel is a two-stage method that outputs ranked candidate correspondences for user inspection. Experiments on public benchmarks and an industrial ERP dataset show that ColRel is particularly effective in semantically related, weak-signal settings.

## [Categorizer Automata for Efficient Discounted-Sum Payoff Binning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26763) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces categorizer automata, a deterministic automaton that reads infinite reward sequences and identifies which of finitely many bins contains the discounted sum. The main contribution is a construction with state space linear in the number of bins, improving over the exponential cross-product of comparator automata. This work advances quantitative synthesis by enabling efficient policy synthesis in Markov decision processes with discontinuous utility functions, which are common in AI applications. The linear state space construction could make these methods scalable to larger problems, potentially impacting areas like reinforcement learning and decision-making under uncertainty. For piecewise-constant utility functions, the algorithm is exact and runs in pseudo-polynomial time; for piecewise-Lipschitz utility functions, it also runs in pseudo-polynomial time and yields an ε-optimal policy. The paper also shows that the synthesis problem is PSPACE-hard even for piecewise-constant utilities.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Quantitative automata extend boolean automata by mapping each execution to a real number, enabling reasoning about quantitative system aspects. Discounted-sum payoffs are a common way to aggregate rewards over infinite horizons, where future rewards are discounted. Markov decision processes (MDPs) provide a framework for decision-making under uncertainty, and synthesizing policies that maximize expected utility is a key problem. Categorizer automata generalize comparator automata, which compare the discounted sum to a threshold, to multiple bins.

This work has the potential to transform control system design from human-driven to agent-driven, significantly improving efficiency and scalability in critical industries such as chemical process control and aero-engine control. It paves the way for advanced control systems like model predictive control to be designed more automatically. The system comprises a Task Modeling Agent that interprets user requirements into engineering constraints, a Controller Design Agent that generates candidate controller structures and executable code, and a Parameter Tuning Agent that refines parameters under closed-loop performance criteria. The experiments demonstrate successful generation of multiple representative control systems, but the paper is an arXiv preprint and has not yet undergone peer review.

The paper introduces SymbolLKG, a neuro-symbolic architecture that integrates a Logical Knowledge Graph (LKG) with dynamic solver routing to enhance verifiable logical reasoning in LLMs. It significantly outperforms state-of-the-art prompting and RAG baselines on logical reasoning benchmarks. The framework uses an ontology-based LKG where logical rules and constraints are treated as first-class topological nodes, enabling explicit modeling of dependencies. ## [C-Unseen: LLM-Based Weak Signal Detection in Dynamic Temporal Knowledge Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26870) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces C-Unseen, a self-interpretable framework for detecting weak signals in Dynamic Temporal Knowledge Graphs (DTKGs). It uses an LLM with chain-of-thought reasoning to extract rare subgraphs and tracks their persistence across time steps to identify true weak signals. This work addresses a gap in existing weak signal detection methods, which often rely on keyword frequency, topic modeling, or untyped graph topology and fail to capture semantic and relational structure. By leveraging LLM reasoning, C-Unseen could improve early detection of significant changes in dynamic knowledge graphs, benefiting applications like trend forecasting and anomaly detection. C-Unseen consists of two modules: a Rare Subgraphs Extractor, which uses an LLM to identify subgraphs that conflict with the dominant snapshot narrative via chain-of-thought reasoning, and a Weak Signal Alerter, which tracks the persistence of these rare subgraphs across time steps. Experimental results show that C-Unseen outperforms keyword-, topic-, and graph-based baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Dynamic Temporal Knowledge Graphs (DTKGs) are knowledge graphs that evolve over time, with each snapshot representing the state at a particular time. Weak signals are early, low-visibility indicators of significant changes, and detecting them is challenging because they are rare and semantically subtle. Chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning is a prompt engineering technique that guides LLMs to reason step-by-step, improving performance on complex tasks. This paper combines these concepts to propose a novel detection framework.

<a id="item-188"></a>
## [LLM vs Human Screening in Scoping Reviews: Recall-Workload Trade-offs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26885) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A preregistered study compared human and LLM title-and-abstract screening workflows in a complex scoping review, finding that no workflow recovered all eligible records. LLM performance varied by workflow configuration, with file-batch runs achieving higher recall than all-at-once runs, and a nominally identical repeat run differed on 94 records. This study provides empirical evidence on the reliability of LLMs in evidence synthesis, highlighting that false negatives remain a critical concern. It underscores that LLM screening performance depends on workflow design, not just model choice, which is crucial for researchers adopting AI in systematic reviews. The study used 1,131 records after a title-only screen, with 316 verified eligible records. Human workflows and two GPT-5.4 file-batch runs retained 42.2-45.0% of records with 82.3-82.9% recall, while Gemini 3.1 file batches achieved 83.9% recall but retained 56.7% of records. The repeat run agreed on 91.7% of records but differed on 94, including 29 eligible records retained by only one run.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Scoping reviews are knowledge syntheses that map the extent and nature of literature on a topic. Title and abstract screening is a labor-intensive step in systematic reviews, and large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used to automate this process. However, false negatives can remove relevant studies before full-text assessment, so evaluating recall is critical. This study uses operational recall, which is based on records advanced to full-text assessment, because final eligibility is only verified for those records.

## [Robust and Fair Learning-Augmented Online Allocation with Unreliable Advice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26889) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a new learning-augmented algorithm for online allocation that combines advice with a conservative fallback and fairness correction, proving consistency and robustness under bounded-error assumptions. Experiments show stability under adversarial advice and significant reductions in exposure disparity. This work addresses the critical challenge of unreliable advice in learning-augmented algorithms, which is essential for practical deployment where predictions are often noisy. It also integrates fairness into online allocation, a growing concern in algorithmic decision-making, potentially impacting fields like advertising and resource allocation. The algorithm handles finite candidate sets, irreversible decisions, and exposure constraints, with theoretical guarantees that loss is proportional to prediction error. Experiments demonstrate reduced exposure disparity and robustness against adversarial advice, though the paper is a specific research contribution rather than a broad breakthrough.

## [LLM-Based Counterfactual Bias Testing for Hiring Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26899) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a reusable methodology using task-specialized LLM agents to generate synthetic resumes and inject controlled demographic treatments across five protected attributes, enabling automated counterfactual bias auditing of candidate-job matching systems. The approach produces a comprehensive fairness report with nine metrics and a PASS/INVESTIGATE/FAIL verdict. As AI hiring systems are increasingly classified as high-risk under regulations like the EU AI Act, this methodology offers a scalable, low-cost complement to traditional correspondence-audit studies, which are expensive and slow. It enables more frequent and thorough bias auditing, potentially improving fairness in automated hiring. The methodology uses a K x (1+N) correspondence-audit matrix, a fine-tuned sentence-embedding model for ranking, and a nine-metric fairness suite including counterfactual, group-fairness, and merit-aware metrics, with bootstrap confidence intervals and Benjamini-Hochberg correction. In an example with 5 job orders, 100 base candidates, and 10 demographic treatments, the rank-stability metric (MARC) and nDCG@K surfaced borderline findings that score- or retention-only views would miss.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Correspondence-audit studies are field experiments where researchers send fabricated resumes to real employers to measure discrimination, but they are labor-intensive and not scalable. The EU AI Act classifies certain AI systems, including those used in recruitment, as high-risk, requiring conformity assessments and bias audits. This paper leverages LLM agents to automate resume generation and demographic treatment injection, making such audits more practical.

## [GraphMemix: Query-Aware Evidence Forests for Multimodal Agent Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26983) ⭐️ 7.0/10

GraphMemix introduces a combinatorial-optimization graph memory framework that models long-term multimodal agent memory as query-aware evidence-forest construction, comprising candidate graph construction, evidence utility and activation costs, and forest optimization. It achieves significant improvements across four long-term multimodal memory benchmarks, establishing a new Pareto frontier between accuracy and lifecycle cost. This work addresses a critical challenge in multimodal agent memory by overcoming the limitations of expensive question-agnostic offline summaries and naive embedding similarity matching. It offers a more efficient and accurate memory organization method, potentially benefiting AI/ML researchers and system developers working on long-horizon agents. The framework decouples direct memory support from anchor-conditioned relation verification to suppress redundant or conflicting information, and jointly selects a forest-format memory context under a maximum evidence budget. It recovers low-similarity complementary evidence by organizing memory into a query-relevant subgraph, avoiding substantial lifecycle costs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal agents require long-term memory to handle complex tasks, but existing methods often rely on expensive offline summaries or simple embedding similarity, which can be incomplete or redundant. Combinatorial optimization is a mathematical approach to find the best solution from a finite set of discrete options, which GraphMemix applies to memory organization. Recent benchmarks like WorldMemArena highlight the importance of memory lifecycle stages—write, maintain, retrieve, use—in evaluating agent memory.

## [Contract-Centered Architecture for Scalable Agentic Runtimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27086) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a contract-centered architecture for enterprise agentic runtimes, introducing four responsibility objects (Skill, Harness, Scaffold, and data substrate) and a falsifiable hypothesis P1 on cost-aware capability-capacity separability. It also outlines a cluster-period randomized crossover experiment to test P1, but no implementation or results are reported. This architecture addresses the coordination and governance challenges in enterprise AI deployment, offering concrete abstractions that could improve scalability and manageability. The falsifiable hypothesis adds rigor to runtime design, potentially guiding future research and engineering practices. The four responsibility objects are Skill (reusable, versioned capability), Harness (runtime compiler and governor), Scaffold (execution/control boundary and NFR owner), and a stack-external data substrate under CIO-governed semantics. The runtime core is A = <s>, with the data substrate outside the stack, and six design conditions become measured obligations for testing P1.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Enterprise AI deployment involves coordination across business units, application teams, testing, platform engineering, infrastructure, security, operations, and data governance. Agentic runtimes are execution engines that run AI agents step by step, persist state, and handle failures, but current benchmarks focus on single-task completion rather than system-level coordination. This paper proposes a contract-based approach to separate concerns and enable scalable management.

It evaluates the framework on bidirectional Chinese-English meme transcreation, achieving a 33.1% average improvement over the strongest baseline in human evaluation and a 60% Top-1 ranking rate in LLM-as-a-Judge. It offers a strong baseline and analysis that can guide future research in multimodal generation and cross-cultural NLP. The framework uses specialized agents for cultural adaptation, target text rewriting, revision, and conditional visual adjustment, coordinated with feedback loops. Error analysis reveals that remaining bottlenecks are humor reconstruction and image-text alignment, not simple cultural knowledge gaps, pointing to future work on humor transfer.

## [GRAIN: RL Framework Boosts LLM Graph Reasoning Robustness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27142) ⭐️ 7.0/10

GRAIN introduces a single-agent reinforcement learning framework with a Structure Invariance Reward to improve LLM robustness to name and narrative shifts in graph reasoning. It also presents GRIT, a new benchmark for evaluating such sensitivity, and outperforms multi-agent baselines by 16.45% in accuracy with ~24% lower latency. This work addresses a critical limitation of LLMs in real-world graph reasoning, where they often overfit to surface patterns and fail under identifier or task shifts. By improving robustness and reducing latency, GRAIN could enable more reliable and efficient deployment of LLM-based graph reasoning in practical applications. GRAIN models reasoning as a semantic parsing and tool-execution pipeline, using the Structure Invariance Reward to validate extracted intermediate graphs against ground-truth topologies. It halves the OOD gap of SFT models from 15.77% to 7.80% and maintains robustness on large-scale graphs beyond the training distribution.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Large Language Models (LLMs) are increasingly used for graph reasoning tasks, but they are brittle to changes in node names or task phrasing, unlike deterministic graph tools. Multi-agent systems can mitigate this but introduce high latency. Reinforcement learning (RL) offers a way to train a single agent to learn robust text-to-structure mappings, and the Structure Invariance Reward ensures the model focuses on topology rather than surface linguistic patterns.

## [HarnessLens: Budget-Aware Harness Evolution via Behavior-Aware Verification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27311) ⭐️ 7.0/10

HarnessLens is a new budget-aware framework for automated harness evolution in language-model agents. It selectively verifies candidate modifications on behavior-relevant tasks using an attributable-evidence gate, improving average held-out performance by 7.6-13.6% while consuming less evaluation budget than baselines. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in agent harness evolution: the high cost of verification. By making verification behavior-aware and budget-aware, HarnessLens enables more efficient and reliable adaptation of agent harnesses, which is essential for deploying LLM agents in production environments where interaction budgets are constrained. HarnessLens jointly explores the task space and user-configurable components, deriving candidate modifications from execution trajectories. It uses an attributable-evidence gate to selectively verify candidates on behavior-relevant tasks, avoiding wasteful rollouts on unrelated behaviors and preventing aggregate scores from obscuring specific regressions. The code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: An agent harness is the software infrastructure that wraps around a language model, handling instructions, tools, and runtime components. Adapting these harnesses typically requires costly verification, and existing propose-and-verify methods score every candidate on a fixed task set, which wastes resources and can miss regressions. HarnessLens improves on this by focusing verification on behavior-relevant tasks and using attribution to guide the process.

## [Position-Based Token Compression Boosts MLLM Referring Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26142) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces PAYN, a plug-and-play, training-free token compression method for MLLM-based referring expression segmentation (RES). PAYN relies solely on position information, retaining tokens distributed across local regions while preserving original positional indices, and outperforms existing compression methods on multiple RES benchmarks. This work addresses a critical computational bottleneck in MLLM-based RES, a task that has seen performance gains from MLLMs but suffers from high overhead. By showing that position information is key for token compression in this task, it offers a practical efficiency solution that could enable broader deployment of MLLMs in real-time segmentation applications. The method is training-free and plug-and-play, meaning it can be easily integrated into existing MLLM-based RES models without additional fine-tuning. Experiments show that preserving original position embeddings and local neighboring spatial structures is crucial, and PAYN outperforms other token compression methods on multiple RES benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Referring Expression Segmentation (RES) is a task that generates pixel-wise segmentation masks from textual descriptions, such as 'the woman in the red dress.' Recent advances in Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have improved RES performance, but their high computational cost limits practical use. Token compression is a strategy to reduce this cost by pruning redundant visual tokens, but it often degrades performance. This paper investigates why and proposes a position-based solution.

## [VFA: Boosting Multilingual MLLMs via Vision-Free Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26155) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Vision-Free Adaptation (VFA), a framework that enhances multilingual capabilities of multimodal large language models (MLLMs) by merging task vectors without requiring non-English image-text data. Experiments on five MLLMs across six multilingual multimodal benchmarks show consistent improvements while preserving general capabilities. This work addresses the critical limitation of English-centric MLLMs by providing a data-efficient method to improve multilingual performance without expensive non-English image-text data. It offers a practical solution for scaling multilingual multimodal models, potentially benefiting global users and reducing the cost of multilingual AI development. VFA fine-tunes a base LLM on multilingual text data to derive a multilingual task vector, then merges it with the vision-aligned task vector of an MLLM. Using less than 2% of the text data, VFA narrows the gap to the fully multimodal-trained model, demonstrating its data efficiency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) combine visual and textual understanding, but most are trained predominantly on English data, limiting their multilingual capabilities. Task vectors are a technique where fine-tuning on a task produces a vector that can be added or subtracted from model weights to steer behavior. Catastrophic forgetting is a common issue where learning new tasks degrades performance on previously learned tasks. VFA leverages task vectors to decouple language and vision alignment, avoiding the need for extensive non-English image-text data.

<a id="item-202"></a>
## [Dual-Seed Comparison Debiases LLM Probabilistic Sampling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26161) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Dual-Seed Comparison (DSC), a tool-free protocol that uses two independent LLM-generated seeds to debias probabilistic sampling. DSC outperforms existing methods in 96% of evaluated settings. This addresses a fundamental limitation in LLMs' ability to generate truly random numbers, which is critical for applications like simulations, cryptography, and unbiased decision-making. The method's simplicity and effectiveness could lead to broader adoption in reliable random generation tasks. DSC compares the character-level ordinal values of two seeds to construct a bit sequence, which is then converted and normalized into a pseudo-uniform variate and mapped to the target distribution via the inverse cumulative distribution function (CDF). Task-adapted variants also improve distributional control in MCQ generation and attribute-constrained text-to-image prompting.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Large Language Models (LLMs) often exhibit systematic biases when generating random variables, warping target probability distributions. Traditional methods rely on a single self-generated seed, which inherits model-specific biases. The inverse cumulative distribution function (CDF) is a standard technique to transform a uniform random variable into a desired distribution, and DSC leverages this by creating a more unbiased uniform variate from two seeds.

## [Poly-Encoders Enable Efficient Automated Creativity Assessment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26165) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a Poly-Encoder-based method for automated creativity assessment, fine-tuned on the Scientific Creative Thinking Test dataset with about 18,000 human-rated responses. It achieves Pearson correlations up to r = 0.74 with human raters, matching LLM performance while significantly reducing computational demands. This work bridges the gap between high performance and computational efficiency, potentially enabling widespread implementation of automated creativity assessment on consumer-grade hardware. It offers a practical, scalable alternative to resource-intensive LLMs, particularly valuable in educational settings. The method leverages small pre-trained BERT encoders and experiments with BERT-family models and poly-code counts. The best result achieved a Pearson correlation of r = 0.74 (95% CI [0.73, 0.75]) with human raters, matching LLM performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Automated creativity assessment has been a long-standing challenge, with traditional methods often being resource-intensive or lacking practical accuracy. The Poly-Encoder is a transformer architecture that balances the speed of bi-encoders with the accuracy of cross-encoders by learning global attention features. The Scientific Creative Thinking Test (SCTT) is a public dataset designed to measure STEM creativity, including subtests on generating hypotheses, research questions, and experimental designs.

## [DRL: Deterministic Relational Middleware for Scalable Enterprise NL2SQL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26172) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces DRL, a deterministic relational middleware layer that prunes context and adds safety checks, achieving up to 92% context reduction on enterprise schemas. It also reveals a regex defect in the evaluation post-processor that had silently suppressed accuracy and manufactured a false cross-vendor gap. This work reframes enterprise NL2SQL as a systems engineering problem, addressing the scalability bottleneck of semantic parsers on large OLTP schemas. The findings could improve the reliability and deployment of natural-language interfaces in real-world enterprise databases, and the benchmark scrutiny highlights the importance of robust evaluation harnesses. On PostgreSQL, DRL's dynamic router achieves a 92% context reduction with pruning p95 = 0.58 ms and middleware p95 = 4.6 ms. GPT-4o, Claude Sonnet 4.5, and Gemini 2.5 Flash achieve 52.9%, 52.8%, and 52.1% execution match under a corrected evaluation harness, with SDop flagging 89-100% of false-positive EX-passing queries.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

Schema-graph scaling refers to the growth in complexity as the number of tables and relationships increases, which can overwhelm LLM attention limits. DRL introduces a middleware layer that dynamically prunes context and adds verification steps to ensure transaction safety.

## [Prompt and Response Language Affect LLM Output Length and Semantics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26186) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new study on arXiv (2608.26186) systematically tested five LLMs across four language combinations (English/Norwegian) and found that prompt language strongly affects response length, with cross-lingual prompts reducing word count by up to 41%, while semantic similarity remains high. This finding is crucial for multilingual AI applications because it shows that prompt language is not neutral—it systematically shapes output length and lexical choices, which can affect user experience and downstream tasks. Developers designing multilingual prompts need to account for these variations to ensure consistent and effective responses. The study used 68 non-translation questions and analyzed 1,348 responses after removing refusals. It measured length differences with Cohen's d, semantic fidelity with LaBSE cosine similarity, and keyword overlap with raw and soft Jaccard indices. Notably, the largest cross-lingual contrast showed a reduction in word count but a smaller reduction in tokens, reflecting tokenizer differences.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Cohen's d is a standardized measure of effect size that quantifies the difference between two means. LaBSE (Language-agnostic BERT Sentence Embedding) is a multilingual model that produces language-agnostic sentence embeddings for 109 languages, enabling cross-lingual semantic similarity comparison. The Jaccard index measures set similarity, and its soft version accounts for partial matches, providing a more nuanced measure of lexical overlap.

## [Chunking Strategy Outweighs Embedding Choice in Turkish RAG Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26192) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper systematically compares three chunking strategies, five embedding models, and two generator LLMs for Turkish RAG, conducting 9,000 graded evaluations with paired McNemar tests under Holm correction. It finds that chunking strategy significantly influences embedding effectiveness, with layout-aware chunking compressing performance differences among modern embedding models to about one point. This study addresses a critical gap for morphologically rich languages like Turkish, where RAG components have not been systematically evaluated. The finding that chunking strategy can outweigh embedding choice has practical implications for NLP practitioners building RAG systems, suggesting that optimizing chunking may yield greater gains than selecting specialized embeddings. The fully crossed design involved three documents with contrasting layouts, and the best configuration reached 87.0% accuracy. Notably, the three leading embedding models were statistically indistinguishable, and the faster generator was not the more accurate one, indicating that language specialization does not yield measurable retrieval advantages.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) combines a retrieval system with a generative model to answer questions using external documents. Chunking refers to how documents are segmented into retrievable units, while embedding models convert text into vector representations for semantic search. For morphologically rich languages like Turkish, these components may behave differently than for English, yet prior research has largely focused on English. The study uses statistical tests like McNemar's test and Holm correction to ensure robust comparisons across many configurations.

## [STeReO: A Reranker for Mixed Speech and Text Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26194) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces STeReO (Speech and Text Reranking Orchestrator), a reranker that combines speech and text retrievers to select the most relevant evidence from mixed-modality databases. It also curates a new dataset with queries, mixed-modality evidence, and relevance ranks to train and evaluate the reranker. This work addresses a growing need in RAG systems to handle databases that contain both speech and text, which is largely unexplored. By improving evidence selection in multimodal RAG, STeReO can enhance the accuracy and reliability of question-answering systems that rely on diverse data sources. STeReO is trained on a curated dataset specifically designed for mixed-modality reranking, and it is evaluated in both single-modality and mixed-modality scenarios. The results show that the proposed algorithm significantly improves downstream question-answering performance by selecting the most relevant evidence.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) systems enhance large language models by retrieving relevant information from external knowledge bases to reduce hallucinations. Traditionally, these databases are text-only, but they are increasingly incorporating other modalities like speech. Reranking is a two-stage retrieval process where an initial set of candidates is re-ordered to improve relevance, and this paper extends it to handle mixed-modality evidence.

A new study on arXiv (2608.26221) empirically tested how prompt modifications and persona names affect generative agents in an epidemic model. It found that synonymous prompts cause negligible changes, but minor variations and contextual changes can influence outcomes, while persona names have no significant impact. This research is crucial for AI safety and behavioral modeling, as it highlights the reliability limitations of using LLM-based agents as human proxies in simulations. It informs researchers that even small prompt tweaks can alter outcomes, which is vital for designing robust agent-based models. The study used a generative agent epidemic model where agents are prompted daily to decide whether to isolate or commingle. The findings indicate that synonymous prompts have negligible impact, but minor variations and contextual changes do affect results, while persona names do not significantly impact epidemic outcomes.

## [LLMs Mirror Human Verbal Probability Biases, Study Finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26327) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new study evaluated 19 large language models on their interpretation of 11 verbal probability expressions, finding that while models largely preserve human word ordering and anchor points, they exhibit a systematic upward bias for negative terms like 'unlikely' and 'improbable'. This research is significant for human-AI interaction, as it reveals that LLMs may systematically misinterpret negative probability phrases, potentially leading to miscommunication in critical domains like healthcare, finance, and risk assessment where precise uncertainty communication is vital. The study used a word-to-number mapping task with two conditions (forced single-number response and explanation elicitation) and a novel bidirectional roundtrip test. Explanation elicitation reduced within-model variance but increased between-model divergence, and frontier models showed better bidirectional consistency.

## [Decay-Region Group Delay: New Forensic Cue for AI-Generated Impulsive Sounds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26346) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces decay-region group delay analysis as a novel forensic cue to distinguish AI-generated impulsive sounds from real ones, achieving an AUC of 0.884 with a Random Forest classifier and 90-94% accuracy with CNN classifiers. This work addresses the growing challenge of AI-generated audio forensics by offering a physically interpretable cue that complements existing magnitude-based methods, potentially improving detection robustness in real-world applications. The study found that AI-generated impulsive sounds show near-identical onset-region group-delay distributions but measurably different decay-region behavior, with KL divergence of 0.322 in decay versus 0.022 in onset. The group delay RF achieved the highest average hold-out accuracy (66.7%) and avoided extreme below-random collapse, though its average AUC (0.731) was lower than CNN (0.762) and AST (0.772).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Group delay is a signal processing concept that measures the delay experienced by different frequency components of a signal as it passes through a system. In audio forensics, distinguishing AI-generated sounds from real ones is crucial for detecting deepfakes and ensuring content authenticity. This paper leverages group delay in the decay region of impulsive sounds, which may carry unique artifacts introduced by generative models.

## [CG4AI: Column Generation Framework for Constrained AI Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26375) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces CG4AI, a novel column generation framework that trains convex combinations of AI models to satisfy linear constraints, demonstrated on MNIST digit classification and multi-commodity flow problems. This addresses a critical gap in standard ML training, which lacks guarantees on output constraints, and could enable safer deployment in domains like autonomous systems and network routing. The framework uses a master linear program to determine optimal mixture weights and a pricing subproblem to generate new models based on LP dual variables, with a cutting-plane procedure extending feasibility guarantees beyond the training set. Experiments show it reliably produces feasible predictors with better accuracy than single-model baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Column generation is a decomposition technique for large-scale linear programs, iteratively adding variables via a pricing subproblem. Linear programming duality provides shadow prices that guide the search for improving variables. This paper applies these optimization concepts to train ensembles of AI models under hard constraints.

## [Simultaneous Envy and Equitability Guarantees in Fair Division](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26410) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper studies the compatibility of envy-freeness and equitability in fair division, showing that EF1+EQ1 may fail to exist even for normalized additive valuations, but providing algorithms for binary goods and stronger guarantees for binary chores. This work bridges two distinct fairness notions and reveals sharp contrasts between goods and chores settings, offering theoretical insights that could influence future algorithm design in AI and economics. The main algorithmic result computes an EF1+EQ1 allocation for normalized binary goods with at most seven agents, while binary chores admit the stronger EFX+EQX guarantee for any number of agents without normalization. The paper also initiates the study of cross-notion ex-ante–ex-post guarantees.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Fair division is the study of allocating resources among agents with fairness guarantees. Envy-freeness (EF) means no agent prefers another's bundle, while equitability (EQ) means all agents value their own bundles equally. Relaxations like EF1 and EQ1 allow for bounded envy or inequity, and binary valuations restrict each item's value to 0 or 1.

## [Latent Diagnostic Taxonomy: A Framework for Trustworthy Prompt Injection Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26423) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces the Latent Diagnostic Taxonomy, a framework for constructing a dimensionality-optimized classifier and diagnosing its decisions, applied to prompt injection detection. It identifies latent support vectors and attack magnitudes to flag prompts that require different treatments. This framework addresses the critical need for trustworthy AI safeguards by providing a systematic way to identify when a classifier's confident decisions can be trusted. It offers actionable strategies for handling different types of failures, which is essential for deploying robust defenses against prompt injection attacks in real-world LLM applications. The framework involves three steps: constructing a classifier with empirically selected embedding dimensionality, locating a small set of latent support vectors (~29% of training examples), and using token attack magnitudes to build a diagnostic taxonomy. Applying it to a public prompt injection dataset, the authors found that ~77% of confident decisions are not robust to removing a single token, revealing two failure patterns: confidence calibration failure and exploitable shortcut.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Prompt injection attacks are a significant security concern for large language models (LLMs), where malicious inputs can manipulate model behavior. Classifiers are often used as safeguard layers to detect such attacks, but their decisions may be brittle. The Latent Diagnostic Taxonomy builds on concepts like support vector machines and diagnostic taxonomies to improve classifier trustworthiness.

## [Manager-Worker Scaffold Boosts LLM Coding for Some Models, Not Others](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26480) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A controlled study introduced a zero-shot manager-worker scaffold over a shared filesystem, tested across nine LLMs on 100 hard LiveCodeBench problems. The scaffold improved scores significantly for some models (e.g., Qwen3.8-27B +23.4, Kimi-K3 +30.4) but had null or negative effects for others (e.g., Qwen3.6-35B -1 to -9). This study provides rigorous, controlled evidence on when multi-agent scaffolding helps, addressing confounded comparisons in prior work. It shows that scaffolding can be a cost-effective alternative to larger models, but its benefits are model-dependent, guiding practitioners in choosing when to apply such techniques. The scaffold roughly triples token usage but often buys accuracy more cheaply than scaling to a larger model; for instance, GPT-5.6-Terra with manager nearly matches Fable 5's single-call accuracy at a fifth of the price. Transcript analysis identified context management and problem decomposition as recurring mechanisms behind gains.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-agent LLM systems often claim to outperform single models, but prior comparisons are confounded by simultaneous changes in prompts, tools, and token budgets. This study isolates the effect of a manager-worker scaffold over a shared filesystem, with no training or per-benchmark tuning. LiveCodeBench is a contamination-free benchmark that continuously collects new coding problems, making it suitable for evaluating generalization.

## [CoGeo-GS: Concept-Driven Multi-Object Removal in 3D Scenes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26656) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CoGeo-GS introduces a concept-driven framework for multi-object removal in 3D scenes using 3D Gaussian Splatting, assigning concept-aware semantic tags to Gaussians and combining monocular depth priors with diffusion-based refinement for geometry completion. It achieves stable and efficient removal in a single optimization stage, outperforming existing methods in visual quality and reconstruction fidelity. This work addresses a challenging problem in 3D scene editing, enabling flexible and controllable removal of multiple objects while maintaining geometric and multi-view consistency. It has potential applications in augmented reality, film production, and 3D content creation, and advances the state of the art in 3D Gaussian Splatting-based editing. The method uses concept-aware semantic tags for flexible object selection and a geometry-aware completion pipeline that combines monocular depth priors with diffusion-based refinement and boundary-aligned blending. A geometry-regularized refinement strategy stabilizes reconstruction and preserves multi-view consistency, as demonstrated in experiments.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) is a rasterization-based technique for real-time radiance field rendering, representing scenes as millions of semi-transparent ellipsoids. It has become popular for 3D reconstruction and editing, but existing methods often struggle with multi-object removal due to occlusions and semantic entanglement. Monocular depth priors provide 3D information from single images, and diffusion-based refinement is used to improve generated content quality.

## [PailitaoGR: Latent Think-with-Images for Generative Image Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26658) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PailitaoGR introduces a novel 'Latent Think-with-Images' method for generative image retrieval, enabling target-focused perception and selective auxiliary evidence utilization without explicit cropping or OCR. It outperforms existing baselines by an average of 13.8% on real-world e-commerce image search logs. This work addresses a key challenge in generative image retrieval: handling diverse real-world query images with mixed content. By internalizing target-focused perception and selective auxiliary evidence utilization, it could significantly improve e-commerce image search accuracy and user experience. The method includes a target-focused perception mechanism with a target Enhancer and on-policy distillation with attention guidance loss, plus a selective auxiliary-evidence utilization mechanism with an auxiliary enhancer and in-capacity incremental contrastive distillation. The training and validation sets are sampled from real-world online image-search logs.

## [PRISM: A New Framework for Evaluating LLM Persona Fidelity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26674) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces PRISM (Persona Reasoning with Inverse SFL-based Modeling), a psycholinguistically grounded framework that reformulates persona fidelity evaluation as a structured inverse inference task. It decomposes persona fidelity into three functional dimensions—Task Framing, Interpersonal Stance, and Linguistic Style—and aggregates dimension-specific evidence into an interpretable and auditable evaluation process. This work addresses a critical gap in LLM evaluation by providing a more reliable and interpretable method for assessing persona fidelity, which is essential for AI alignment and human-AI interaction. It could influence future evaluation methodologies and improve the deployment of LLMs in roles that require consistent character simulation. PRISM is inspired by Systemic Functional Linguistics (SFL) and estimates dimension-specific evidence over a persona-conditioned label space. Experiments show that PRISM yields more accurate and stable judgments than traditional holistic judging, which is prone to 'holistic appraisal hallucination'.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Systemic Functional Linguistics (SFL) is an approach to linguistics that views language as a social semiotic system, emphasizing how language functions to construe experience, enact social relations, and create text. In LLM evaluation, persona fidelity refers to how consistently an agent's behavior reflects the psychological and stylistic traits of a target persona. Existing methods either use holistic LLM-based judges, which can hallucinate, or static psychometric inventories, which fail to capture context-dependent fidelity in dynamic dialogues.

## [AesCanvas: New Benchmark for Aesthetic Critique and Contextual Suitability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26713) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AesCanvas introduces a large-scale dataset and benchmark with two components: CritiqueCanvas, containing 519,136 instruction-response pairs from 54,300 images, and ContextCanvas, featuring 301 expert-reviewed use scenarios. It evaluates MLLMs on both long-form aesthetic critique and contextual suitability, revealing a gap between critique generation and context-sensitive judgment. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in MLLM evaluation by moving beyond intrinsic visual quality to assess whether an image is appropriate for specific purposes, audiences, and cultural contexts. The findings suggest that aesthetic specialization does not reliably transfer to contextual suitability, highlighting the need for culturally situated and evidence-grounded aesthetic modeling. The study evaluates closed-source frontier, open-weight general, and aesthetic-specific MLLMs under a unified protocol. Results show that reference-based lexical and semantic metrics only partially capture critique quality, and aesthetic specialists lag behind strong general-purpose MLLMs on ContextCanvas, with model decisions often failing to track decisive contextual visual cues.

## [LiveVVT: Real-Time High-Fidelity Video Virtual Try-On](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26714) ⭐️ 7.0/10

LiveVVT introduces a rolling streaming diffusion framework for video virtual try-on that achieves real-time generation with high fidelity. It preserves bounded bidirectional modeling within a causal recurrent generation process, using complementary temporal and global appearance memories to maintain consistency. This work addresses the critical latency and computational overhead that hinder practical deployment of video virtual try-on systems. By enabling real-time streaming with 26x lower latency and 11x higher throughput, it could make interactive virtual try-on feasible for e-commerce and live applications. LiveVVT uses a fixed-size window to jointly denoise multiple video chunks with bounded look-ahead, emitting one clean chunk per iteration. It also introduces a progressive distillation framework that integrates bidirectional VVT learning, teacher-trajectory regression, and Collaborative Matching Distillation to align optimization with recurrent inference.

**Background**: Diffusion-based video virtual try-on (VVT) models achieve high visual fidelity through bidirectional spatio-temporal modeling, but they require processing the entire video clip, leading to high latency and computational cost. Causal generation reduces latency but often degrades quality because it disrupts pretrained bidirectional priors. LiveVVT balances these trade-offs by maintaining bounded bidirectional interactions within a rolling window and using memories to preserve long-term consistency.

## [Rethinking GNN Message Passing as Retrieval with RTA Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26732) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a retrieval-augmented view of GNNs, where each layer applies an MLP to a node representation and a permutation-invariant summary of retrieved graph context. It introduces RTA, a simple MLP-based framework that replaces structural message passing with label-aware retrieval and propagation, and provides theoretical insights connecting retrieval-based aggregation to softmax-attention message passing. This work challenges the conventional message-passing paradigm in GNNs, offering a more efficient and robust alternative for text-attributed graph learning. If validated, RTA could reduce computational costs and improve resilience to imperfect graph structures, impacting applications like node classification and link prediction on text-rich graphs. RTA matches or outperforms strong GNN and graph LLM baselines on multiple text-attributed graph benchmarks while improving efficiency and robustness. The theoretical analysis establishes a connection between retrieval-based aggregation and softmax-attention message passing, and proves robustness to mis-retrieved outliers.

**Background**: Graph neural networks (GNNs) typically use message passing to aggregate neighborhood information, but this can be computationally expensive and sensitive to graph structure quality. Text-attributed graphs (TAGs) combine text and graph structure for tasks like classification and link prediction. Retrieval-augmented learning, inspired by retrieval-based models, offers a non-parametric alternative that can be more efficient and robust.

## [FaultLens: Learning Compact Behavioral Test Suites for Generated Programs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26746) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FaultLens is a new method that learns compact behavioral test suites for generated operational programs by exploiting fault-probe kill relations and diversity. It balances coverage and cost, achieving 99.0% coverage of dynamically killable faults using only 1.2-2.0% of the exhaustive domain. This work addresses a practical trade-off between sparse hand-written tests and expensive exhaustive regression suites for validating generated programs. It could significantly reduce testing costs while maintaining high fault detection, benefiting software testing and validation in automated program generation contexts. The method executes a rich probe domain once, stores the fault-probe kill relation as a sparse outcome cache, and learns probe orderings from earlier program generations. It combines a fault-driven greedy component with a mutation-independent diversity component, and in a downstream deployment study, a conservative admission rule reduced severe tail regressions from 15/20 to 0/20 program-environment groups.

**Background**: Generated operational programs are often validated with either a few hand-written examples or exhaustive regression suites. The former can miss sparse boundary and interaction faults, while the latter can be unnecessarily expensive. Mutation testing is a related technique that evaluates test quality by introducing small changes (mutants) and checking if tests detect them. FaultLens builds on these concepts to learn compact test suites that maintain high fault detection while minimizing cost.

MedFG-VQA introduces a lightweight medical VQA framework combining DCT-based low-frequency memory fusion and graph-enhanced cross-attention, achieving competitive performance with lower computational cost. It also presents SynMed-VQA, a large synthetic dataset with over 2 million QA pairs across 9 modalities and 10 organs, generated using GPT-4o. This work addresses two critical challenges in medical VQA: data scarcity and high computational demands of large models. The framework comprises two main components: Frequency-Memory Fusion (FMF) enhances low-frequency features via a learnable memory bank built on DCT decomposition, and Graph-Aware Cross-Attention (GACA) aligns visual-textual features with graph-convolutional refinement. ## [CoVeMem: Collaborative Vector Memory for Agentic Recommenders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26895) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CoVeMem replaces text-based memory in agentic recommender systems with a collaborative vector memory, using frozen LightGCN states as a memory bank and soft tokens to inject relevant historical states into the LLM context. It matches or exceeds the strongest text-memory agent on 19 of 20 metric cells across four benchmarks, with zero additional LLM calls for memory maintenance. This addresses two key limitations of text-based memory: scalability (updating one rewrite at a time is expensive) and loss of collaborative evidence (graded similarity over the catalog doesn't survive translation to sentences). By making the full interaction history available as training data, CoVeMem enables more efficient and collaborative-aware recommendations, potentially advancing LLM-integrated recommender systems. The memory bank consists of frozen LightGCN user and item states; at each decision, the candidate set retrieves the most relevant historical states, which enter the LLM context as soft tokens alongside a light textual profile. Training involves contrastive alignment to item-semantic anchors and listwise co-training with masked candidates, plus a pointwise yes/no readout for scoring.

**Background**: Agentic recommender systems use LLMs to make decisions grounded in a persistent user memory, typically maintained as text narratives. LightGCN is a graph convolutional network for collaborative filtering that learns user and item embeddings via linear propagation. Soft tokens are learnable embeddings that can be inserted into an LLM's context to provide additional information without natural language.

## [Training-Free Filtering Removes Transient Objects in Feed-Forward 3DGS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26951) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a training-free filtering procedure that leverages per-view Gaussian predictions to remove transient objects from feed-forward 3D Gaussian Splatting reconstructions. The method operates on a single frozen prediction without retraining or scene-specific optimization, and consistently improves novel-view quality across three reconstruction models and two distractor benchmarks. This work addresses a practical problem in 3D reconstruction from casual captures, where transient objects can cause artifacts. By providing a training-free solution, it enhances the accessibility and robustness of feed-forward 3DGS, potentially benefiting applications in AR/VR, robotics, and content creation. The filtering procedure excludes each input's associated Gaussians and renders the same camera using the remaining representation to reveal inconsistent content. Feature similarity forms candidate regions, and rendering-based verification retains only candidates whose removal reduces reconstruction error in other input views. On clean scenes, evaluations across four models show that original reconstructions are largely preserved.

However, transient objects appearing in only a subset of views can be encoded into per-view Gaussians and cause artifacts in novel views. Existing methods like T-3DGS require training or optimization, whereas this work proposes a training-free alternative.

## [OCSD: Object-Conditioned Social Diffusion for Multi-Person Motion Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27039) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Object-Conditioned Social Diffusion (OCSD), a conditional diffusion model that integrates motion history, multi-person interactions, and object cues into a unified framework for multi-person motion forecasting in complex scenes. It achieves state-of-the-art results on the Humans in Kitchens (HiK) and HOI-M3 benchmarks, reducing two-second path error by 121.5 mm (31.3%) on HiK and 130.5 mm (33.2%) on HOI-M3 compared to prior work. This work addresses a critical challenge in human motion forecasting by effectively incorporating object information and social interactions, which is essential for applications like robotics and autonomous systems. The significant error reduction and ability to generate realistic long-term forecasts could advance the state of the art and enable more reliable human-robot interaction in dynamic environments. OCSD uses an object-conditioning mechanism that modulates denoising at every timestep, enabling fine-grained human-object reasoning, and a social encoder that models interactions between all humans in the scene. The model naturally handles varying group sizes, complex social interactions, and supports sampling multiple plausible futures.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Human motion forecasting is a key problem in computer vision, with applications in robotics, autonomous driving, and surveillance. Traditional methods often struggle to incorporate object interactions and social dynamics, but diffusion models, a class of generative models that iteratively denoise data, have shown promise in generating realistic and diverse predictions. The HiK dataset is a large-scale multi-person motion dataset with annotated 3D poses and scene geometry, providing a challenging benchmark for this task.

## [Beyond Classification: Compute-Aware Multi-Task PET Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27066) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a compute-aware multi-task evaluation protocol for privacy-enhancing image transformations, using lightweight proxy tasks to assess task-dependent learnability beyond classification. The protocol is demonstrated across irreversible privacy transformations, key-based block primitives, and learnable image encryption schemes. This work addresses a critical gap in evaluating privacy-enhancing technologies (PETs) for computer vision, as classification-only metrics are insufficient for generic vision tasks. The proposed protocol offers a practical, compute-efficient alternative to exhaustive downstream-task evaluation, potentially influencing how PETs are assessed and developed in the future. The protocol combines lightweight proxy tasks that target complementary aspects of visual structure, remaining simple and fast to compute. The paper demonstrates that PETs with similar classification accuracy can differ substantially on other tasks, highlighting the need for multi-task evaluation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Privacy-enhancing technologies (PETs) in computer vision often use noise or image perturbations to protect visual data, creating a trade-off between task performance and protection. Traditionally, this trade-off is evaluated using image classification, which primarily captures semantic separability and remains robust to geometric or local boundary changes, making it too simplistic for generic vision tasks. Exhaustive downstream-task evaluation is computationally expensive because models must be trained for each PET transformation and parameter setting.

## [Emotional Preferences Regulate Goal Priorities in RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27072) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a framework where emotional preferences, autonomously generated by high-level goals, regulate the priorities of competing lower-level objectives in reinforcement learning agents. It proposes a two-level architecture with an inner multi-objective RL controller and an outer preference generator, and demonstrates that learned preference functions outperform fixed or handcrafted strategies in multi-objective exploration environments. This work bridges psychology and AI by computationally realizing the goal-directed theory of emotion, potentially enabling more adaptive and autonomous decision-making in complex systems. It addresses a core question in agent design: how to dynamically prioritize objectives without external specification, which could impact fields like robotics, autonomous vehicles, and personalized AI assistants. The framework consists of an inner controller that provides preference-conditioned behaviors and an outer generator that learns a mapping from state to preferences via RL on a high-level goal. The authors derive an upper bound on the optimality gap that vanishes when the optimal policy is representable by the preference-conditioned policies, and experiments show contextual priority switching, graded trade-offs, and temporal persistence.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning (RL) trains agents to maximize rewards through interactions with an environment, often involving multiple conflicting objectives. Multi-objective reinforcement learning (MORL) generalizes RL to handle such trade-offs, but typically requires predefined preferences. The goal-directed theory of emotion, proposed by psychologist Agnes Moors, suggests that emotions arise from decision-making processes aimed at achieving goals, providing a theoretical basis for autonomously generating preferences.

## [Active Diffusion-Based Inference for Ill-Posed Inverse Problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27080) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes an active diffusion-based inverse problem solver that iteratively detects and corrects model misspecification using posterior uncertainty, enabling robust inference even when initial training bounds exclude the true parameters. It demonstrates effectiveness on a toy problem with infinite solutions and on a Quantum Chromodynamics analysis of nucleon structure. This work addresses a critical limitation in diffusion-based inverse problem solvers: handling incomplete priors and model misspecification. It provides a principled Bayesian framework for adaptive domain augmentation, potentially improving reliability in scientific computing and machine learning applications where prior knowledge is incomplete. The method trains a diffusion model to learn the mapping between parameter and observable spaces, then iteratively corrects for model misspecification via posterior uncertainty. It provides a Bayesian justification for adaptive domain augmentation and is validated on a toy inverse problem with infinite solutions and a Quantum Chromodynamics parameterization task.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Inverse problems involve estimating unknown parameters from observable data, which is challenging due to nonlinearity, noise, and ill-posedness. Diffusion models are generative models that learn data distributions and have been adapted for inverse problems, but often assume complete priors. This paper introduces an active approach that adaptively expands the domain to handle incomplete priors, grounded in Bayesian inference.

## [ContraTalk Benchmark Exposes Transcript Shortcuts in Audio-Grounded Dialogue](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27176) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces ContraTalk, a benchmark with 501 questions across five discourse dimensions, designed to test models on cross-modal disagreement where transcripts mislead. It also proposes an agentic framework that converts speech into an Audio Twin, a text-readable representation of acoustic cues, to improve reasoning. This work addresses a critical gap in audio-grounded dialogue evaluation by formalizing cross-modal disagreement and exposing transcript-based shortcuts, which are a common failure mode in multimodal AI. The benchmark and framework could drive progress in speech understanding and multimodal reasoning, impacting fields like human-computer interaction and assistive technologies. Strong text-only LLMs achieve over 90% accuracy on consistent cases but drop to 33-48% on conflict cases, while direct AudioLLMs still select the transcript-biased trap in 30-40% of conflicts. The Audio Twin framework improves conflict-case accuracy but its consistent-case performance remains backbone-dependent.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Audio-grounded dialogue requires joint reasoning over lexical content and paralinguistic signals like emotion and intent. Existing evaluations often allow shortcuts based on transcripts, obscuring whether models truly understand speech. Cross-modal disagreement occurs when transcripts suggest plausible but incorrect interpretations, while acoustic cues support different answers. This benchmark formalizes this failure mode to encourage more robust speech-grounded reasoning.

## [RCMN Framework Unveils Reader-Centric Misleadingness in Public Discourse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27358) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces the Reader-Centric Misleadingness Understanding (RCMN) framework, which operationalizes misleadingness through five dimensions: misleading mechanism, likely reader interpretation, evidence-warranted interpretation, emotional arousal, and communicative intent. It also constructs an evidence-grounded dataset of influential public discourse and evaluates five generative foundation models on this task. This work addresses an underexplored aspect of misinformation—how misleadingness arises beyond fabrication through framing, omission, and context. It provides a framework and dataset that could improve AI and NLP systems' ability to detect and understand misleading content, with implications for media analysis and public discourse monitoring. Empirical findings show that misleadingness is diverse, with unsupported inference, exaggeration, and omission being prevalent mechanisms, often associated with heightened emotional arousal and distortive communicative intent. The evaluation across five recent generative foundation models reveals that reader-level interpretations can often be recovered from lightweight claim-and-context representations, but identifying how misleadingness is produced remains challenging.

**Background**: Influential public discourse shapes beliefs and can mislead through framing, omission, and context, not just false statements. Traditional fact-checking focuses on claim-level factuality, but misleadingness is a broader concept that requires understanding reader interpretations and communicative intent. The RCMN framework aims to fill this gap by providing a reader-centric operationalization and an evidence-grounded dataset.

## [KnockGS: Calibrating Material Scales in 3D Gaussian Physics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27365) ⭐️ 7.0/10

KnockGS introduces a framework that estimates the elasticity and density scales of 3D Gaussian objects from their dynamics under known applied forces, turning force-induced responses into calibration signals. This enables accurate physics simulation without manual material specification. This addresses a critical gap in physics-integrated 3D Gaussian representations, where material parameters were previously assumed known or manually set. It could significantly impact robotics, simulation, and interactive applications by enabling automatic material calibration from observed dynamics. The framework extracts temporal response features from observed dynamics, estimates the two material scales, and freezes the estimate back into the simulator for testing on unseen interactions. Evaluations show it recovers scales more accurately than response retrieval, global regression, or fixed defaults, and remains predictive under different interaction directions and magnitudes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: 3D Gaussian splatting is a rendering technique that represents scenes as millions of semi-transparent ellipsoids, enabling real-time photorealistic rendering. Physics-integrated 3D Gaussian representations extend this by simulating deformable objects under explicit material models, but they typically require known material parameters. Material calibration is a common process in computational science to adjust model parameters to match observations, and KnockGS applies this concept to 3D Gaussian physics.

## [Successive Capacity Growth for JEPA Vision Transformer Encoders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27367) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Successive Capacity Growth (SCG), a method that dynamically expands Vision Transformer encoders in JEPA world models based on task complexity, using function-preserving expansion and a regularizer to maintain semantic independence. SCG addresses the inefficiency of fixed-size encoders in JEPA world models, potentially reducing compute and data requirements while maintaining representation quality. This could lead to more adaptive and efficient world models for complex tasks. SCG starts from a minimal encoder (1 head, 2 layers, 283K parameters) and grows in width (adding attention heads) or depth (adding transformer blocks) via a test-and-verify mechanism. It uses the Sketched Isotropic Gaussian Regularizer (SIGReg) to prevent collapse, achieving up to 23% improvement over fixed large models with zero false-positive expansions.

**Background**: Joint-Embedding Predictive Architectures (JEPAs) are self-supervised learning frameworks that predict latent representations of future inputs. Vision Transformers (ViTs) are commonly used as encoders in such models, but fixed-size encoders are often over- or under-provisioned for different task complexities. Function-preserving expansion allows neural networks to grow without changing their output, and SIGReg is a regularizer that encourages isotropic Gaussian latent embeddings to prevent representation collapse.

## [Beyond F1: Evaluating Coverage and Failure Recovery in AI Model Security Scanners](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27424) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a synthetic benchmark of 170 Pickle and PyTorch artifacts across 145 specimen families to evaluate three AI model security scanners—ModelScan, ModelAudit, and Fickling—using novel metrics of coverage and failure recovery beyond traditional F1. The results show that ModelAudit achieved 100% definitive security decisions, while ModelScan only 49.6%, and Fickling 81.5%, highlighting significant differences in judgment availability. This work addresses a critical gap in evaluating AI model security scanners by separating judgment accuracy from judgment availability, which is essential for practitioners who rely on these tools to protect against malicious model files. The findings underscore the need for more comprehensive evaluation metrics and highlight the practical limitations of current scanners, potentially influencing future tool development and security practices. On labeled families, ModelAudit produced definitive security decisions for all 135 families (100%), Fickling for 110 (81.5%), and ModelScan for 67 (49.6%). Conditional on making a definitive judgment, ModelScan achieved 100% precision, recall, and F1, but it failed to complete analysis on 48 malicious families where both ModelAudit and Fickling generated detections consistent with ground truth.

**Background**: AI model security scanners are static analysis tools that inspect machine learning artifacts, such as Pickle and PyTorch files, for executable or unsafe content that could lead to code execution or other attacks. Traditional evaluation metrics like F1 score only consider cases where a scanner produces a usable security judgment, ignoring scenarios where the scanner fails to provide a definitive answer. This paper introduces a benchmark that explicitly distinguishes between non-N/A coverage, analysis completion, definitive security decisions, non-security findings, and unsupported outcomes, providing a more nuanced view of scanner performance.

## [Persona-Execution Separation: A New Architecture Pattern for Auditable LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27427) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Persona-Execution Separation (PES), an architecture pattern that places LLM agent persona and execution in different trust domains connected by a governed contract bridge, allowing persona drift while keeping execution auditable. It also provides a formal argument that any single-domain mechanism meeting all three goals must reintroduce typed change objects, an external gate, and a stable audit anchor. This pattern addresses a critical tension in governed organizations that deploy LLM agents: the need for persona evolution versus the requirement for execution auditability. By separating these concerns, PES offers a practical way to deploy agents in regulated environments without compromising either flexibility or compliance, which could influence how future agent systems are architected. The paper includes a development/pilot case in a regulated digital-employee platform, documenting five decisions over one month, each with a rejected alternative. A mechanism check on the shipped implementation found no execution-side re-validation under persona perturbation (five model configurations) and no persona fingerprint on hard-asserted fields; a probe of a recovered pre-separation build found the governed execution path decoupled from the persona by omission, not by construction.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents in governed organizations need to evolve their persona (instructions, tone, self-presentation) while keeping execution (stateful, audited work) traceable. A single trust domain cannot satisfy both cheaply. PES separates persona and execution into different trust domains, connected by a governed contract bridge, with an approval matrix, DLP, and audit enforcing the crossing. The pattern applies when multi-user deployment, execution audit, and expected persona churn hold jointly.

It outperforms fixed and agentic baselines on multiple benchmarks, improves tool efficiency, and transfers across attacker models and target execution harnesses. This work addresses a critical gap in LLM agent security by moving beyond fixed attacks and full-trajectory retrieval, offering a more interpretable and efficient approach to jailbreak detection in product-level environments. It could significantly improve automated red-teaming for LLM-based agents, which are increasingly deployed in real-world applications where jailbreaks can cause harmful tool use and persistent state changes. The attack skill evolves through tool-effectiveness profiling and Deciding-Tool Attribution, with a validation ratchet that retains only updates improving validation performance. Experiments were conducted on multiple benchmarks, target models, and target execution harnesses, showing improved tool efficiency and transferability across attacker models and target execution harnesses.

**Background**: LLM-based agents are increasingly deployed in product-level execution harnesses, where jailbreaks can trigger harmful tool use and persistent state changes, creating greater risks than unsafe text generation alone. <a id="item-244"></a>
## [SWE-Prime: Fewer Trajectories, Better Performance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27449) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SWE-Prime introduces a multi-granularity, two-stage data selection method that filters high-quality agent trajectories for supervised fine-tuning (SFT), improving LLM performance on software engineering tasks. Experiments on SWE-Bench Pro and SWE-Bench Verified show that training on a 10% trajectory subset selected by SWE-Prime outperforms training on the full resolved dataset, with relative gains of up to 12.2% and 24.2%. This work addresses the critical issue of noisy supervision in SFT for software engineering agents, showing that data quality matters more than quantity. It could lead to more efficient and effective fine-tuning practices, reducing computational costs while improving model performance, which is significant for the AI/ML and software engineering communities. The method operates in two stages: trajectory-level screening based on process quality, result quality, and data representativeness, followed by segment-level selection that groups consecutive steps into semantic segments and assesses each segment's contribution, learnability, and risks. During SFT, all segments remain in the sequence to preserve context, but only selected segments contribute to the loss computation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Supervised fine-tuning (SFT) on successful agent trajectories is a common approach to improve LLMs' ability to solve real-world software issues. However, successful trajectories may contain ineffective, redundant, or risky steps, leading to noisy supervision. SWE-Prime addresses this by filtering trajectories and segments based on quality and representativeness, aiming to select the most beneficial training data.
