## [LLM-Powered Swarms: Emulating Swarm Intelligence with High Overhead](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.14496) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2506.14496) evaluates whether LLM-powered swarm systems like OpenAI's Swarm framework truly capture classical swarm intelligence principles. The authors implemented Boids and Ant Colony Optimization using OpenAI's Swarm and found that while LLM-based swarms can emulate swarm-like dynamics, they incur roughly 300x more computational time than classical counterparts. This research highlights a critical limitation of applying LLM-driven swarms to real-time systems, given the substantial computational overhead. It also raises important questions about whether the 'swarm' label is conceptually appropriate for LLM-based multi-agent systems, which could influence future research directions in multi-agent AI. The study used OpenAI's Swarm (OAS) framework, which is now deprecated in favor of the OpenAI Agents SDK. The experiments compared classical and LLM-based versions of Boids and Ant Colony Optimization, with the LLM-based Boids simulation requiring roughly 300x more computation time. The paper is a preprint and has not yet undergone peer review.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Swarm intelligence refers to the collective behavior of decentralized, self-organized systems, such as bird flocks or ant colonies. Classical algorithms like Boids and Ant Colony Optimization simulate these behaviors using simple rules and have been widely used in optimization and simulation. Recently, LLM-powered frameworks like OpenAI's Swarm have emerged, where agents coordinate through natural language, but their computational cost and fidelity to classical principles are not well understood.

## [Do LLMs Follow Occam's Razor? New Framework Evaluates Parsimony in Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.03345) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a framework to synthetically generate reasoning questions that require both inductive and abductive reasoning, and proposes a new automated metric to assess whether LLM hypotheses adhere to Occam's Razor. The study evaluates state-of-the-art LLMs and finds they struggle with complex world models and producing high-quality, parsimonious hypotheses. This work addresses a previously ignored aspect of non-deductive reasoning evaluation in LLMs, providing a novel framework and metric that could influence future benchmarks and model development. Understanding whether LLMs adhere to Occam's Razor is crucial for improving their reasoning reliability in real-world applications. The framework is readily extendable to produce any abductive/inductive reasoning question expressible in first-order logic. The findings indicate that even with reasoning-enhancing techniques like in-context learning and RLVR, LLMs struggle with complex world models and producing high-quality hypotheses.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Non-deductive reasoning includes inductive reasoning, which draws general conclusions from specific observations, and abductive reasoning, which infers the most plausible explanation for given observations. Occam's Razor is a principle that favors simpler hypotheses over complex ones, and it is often applied in machine learning to avoid overfitting. First-order logic is a formal system used to express reasoning tasks, allowing for systematic generation of test questions.

## [TabGR: Graph-Based Table Reasoning Outperforms Linearization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.08444) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Table Graph Reasoner (TabGR), a training-free model that represents tables as Attributed Table Graphs (ATG) and uses a Question-Guided Personalized PageRank (QG-PPR) mechanism to rerank tabular data. TabGR consistently outperforms state-of-the-art models by up to 9.7% in accuracy across multiple table reasoning benchmarks. This work addresses critical limitations in LLM-based table reasoning, such as the lost-in-the-middle issue and lack of traceable evidence paths, by preserving table structure and enabling graph-based reasoning. It could improve both performance and interpretability, impacting applications that rely on tabular data understanding. TabGR is training-free and explicitly preserves row-column-cell structure in the ATG, enabling traceable reasoning paths. The QG-PPR mechanism mitigates the lost-in-the-middle issue by reranking tabular data based on the question. Code is available at https://github.com/yxw-11/TabGR.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Table reasoning is a task where models answer questions by reasoning over tabular data. Traditional approaches linearize tables into text for LLMs, which can lose structural information and suffer from the lost-in-the-middle phenomenon, where models perform worse when key information is in the middle of a long context. Attributed Table Graphs (ATG) represent tables as graphs with attributed nodes and edges, preserving structure and enabling graph-based reasoning. Personalized PageRank is a graph algorithm that measures node importance relative to a set of seed nodes, which QG-PPR adapts for question-guided reranking.

The paper notes that the number of unique DTCs in a modern vehicle is on the same order of magnitude as a natural language vocabulary, often in the tens of thousands. It unifies event sequence modeling, causal discovery, and large language models (LLMs) into a coherent framework, structured in three parts: prediction, causal understanding, and reasoning.

Nomad introduces an exploration-first architecture that constructs an explicit Exploration Map over a domain and uses explorer agents to systematically traverse it, generating hypotheses and insights. It includes an independent verifier and a reporting pipeline that produces cited reports and meta-reports, with a comprehensive evaluation framework. This addresses a key limitation of current query-driven and prompt-driven research systems, which are constrained by human framing and often miss broader insights. Nomad could enable more autonomous and comprehensive data analysis, benefiting researchers and analysts across fields. The system uses document search, web search, and database tools within explorer agents, and candidate insights are checked by an independent verifier before reporting. Evaluation on UN/WHO reports and arXiv papers on LLM agents shows strong numeric grounding, higher quality and actionability than baselines, and more diverse insights.

## [HCRC Framework Reduces LLM Epistemic Entropy via Verification Gates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04562) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Heaviside Continuity of Rolling Coefficients (HCRC), a verification-first execution framework that gates LLM state transitions on correctness predicates, reducing epistemic entropy without modifying the model. Evaluated on software-engineering and reasoning tasks, HCRC reduces false-completion rates from 4–7% to 0% on capable proposers. This framework addresses a critical limitation of autoregressive decoding—lack of intermediate verification—by providing a general, model-agnostic control mechanism. It demonstrates that reliable reasoning can be achieved through execution control rather than model scale, potentially improving LLM reliability in production agentic systems. HCRC uses a Heaviside Gate to combine model confidence with independent verification signals from a parallel worker architecture, allowing execution to advance only when predefined correctness predicates are satisfied. It has been deployed as a production control plane for an agentic coding environment for months, authorizing file mutations and verification-driven progress reporting.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) generate fluent outputs that can be wrong, but autoregressive decoding provides no mechanism for verifying intermediate reasoning, making errors hard to detect. The Heaviside step function is a mathematical function that is zero for negative arguments and one for positive arguments, often used in control systems to represent a switch. Epistemic entropy refers to uncertainty or information loss in model outputs, which can indicate hallucination risk. Predicate-gated state transitions are a concept from formal verification where state changes are allowed only if certain conditions hold.

This paper presents an ablation study of an ARC-AGI-3 agent, comparing four Codex-based variants to isolate the contributions of executable world modeling, prompted simplification, and exact replay verification. The study finds that model capability and reasoning effort often outweigh the benefits of these mechanisms, with the verification variant ranking first but using more resources. This research provides empirical insights into the design of AI agents for ARC-AGI benchmarks, suggesting that simpler approaches may be as effective as complex mechanisms. The findings could influence future agent architectures and resource allocation in AI development. The study evaluated variants with gpt-5.4 and gpt-5.5 at high and xhigh reasoning effort on 25 public games, with exploratory follow-ups using gpt-5.6-sol. The verification variant completed all public levels at xhigh and max with about 99% human-relative action efficiency, while the textual variant at max used 41% fewer actions than the human baseline.

## [High-Weight Neurons Not Always Key in Image Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25529) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2607.25529) empirically investigates the relationship between high-weight neurons and functionally important neurons in image classification networks, finding only partial overlap and nuanced perturbation effects. Experiments on CIFAR-10 and Mini-ImageNet reveal that top 10% high-weight neurons overlap with important ones by only about 25% at maximum. This challenges the common assumption that weight magnitude indicates neuron importance, which is crucial for pruning, backdoor defense, and interpretability. The findings suggest that pruning strategies based solely on weights may inadvertently remove critical low-weight neurons, impacting model performance and security. The study uses three experiments: overlap quantification, perturbation analysis, and ablation-retraining. Perturbation tests show top 10% high-weight neurons cause 45-80% accuracy degradation under certain operations, but a third show minimal impact; ablating top 10% leaves accuracy 10-20% below baseline with no recovery, while top 0.1% allows near-full recovery. Notably, some low-weight intervals show 10-17% degradation when perturbed, comparable to mid-range high-weight neurons.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Neuron importance is a key concept in neural network interpretability and pruning, where neurons with larger weights are often assumed to be more important. However, this assumption lacks systematic validation. The paper addresses this by proposing a neuron importance assessment method using overlap, perturbation, and ablation-retraining experiments on image classification models.

This paper introduces MRCF, a Modality Reliability-Calibrated Framework for multimodal sentiment analysis with incomplete observations. It explicitly models modality reliability through a Reliability-Aware Branch, a Reliability-Guided Interaction Branch, and a Reliability-Calibrated Fusion Module, achieving strong performance on CMU-MOSI, CMU-MOSEI, and CH-SIMS. This work addresses a critical challenge in multimodal sentiment analysis—handling incomplete data—by explicitly modeling modality reliability, which mitigates reliability mismatch and propagation bias. It could influence future research in multimodal learning and improve robustness in real-world applications where data is often missing. MRCF estimates sample-specific modality reliability from intramodal quality cues and cross-modal semantic consistency, then uses these scores to modulate cross-modal information flow and integrate reliability with semantic cues for prediction. Experiments show that explicit reliability modeling helps mitigate the two identified issues during interaction and fusion.

## [NiyamAI: Zero-Knowledge Proofs for Verifiable LLM Agent Guardrails](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07167) ⭐️ 7.0/10

NiyamAI introduces an intent-bound runtime guardrail architecture that uses SHA256 commitments and zkSNARK proofs to provide cryptographically verifiable execution integrity for autonomous LLM agents. In tests on 2,000 AgentSafetyBench scenarios, it achieved 88.8% F1 at a 1.0% false positive rate, outperforming existing guardrails. This addresses a critical security gap in LLM agents by making guardrail enforcement externally verifiable, which is essential for high-stakes deployments. It could set a new standard for trustworthy AI agent execution, impacting industries relying on autonomous decision-making. The architecture includes a deterministic authority gate and a neural Judge (11->8->2 feedforward network) that classifies tool invocations. Proof generation adds 1.7 seconds per approved action, verification takes 51 ms, and the 18.6 KB proof can be verified by any third party without model parameters.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Autonomous LLM agents can execute tools, but they are vulnerable to prompt injection and goal hijacking. Traditional guardrails rely on unverified local software that shares the agent's environment, offering no cryptographic guarantee. Zero-knowledge proofs allow one party to prove a statement's truth without revealing underlying data, and SHA256 commitments ensure data integrity.

FlavourBench introduces a culinary-themed benchmark that uses executable reward maps (dense answer maps) to evaluate and post-train language models, testing 27 models on 534 tasks with 14,418 observations. The paper also reports a preregistered post-training study where LoRA SFT on a pinned Qwen3-0.6B checkpoint improved scores by 13.30 points over a control. The benchmark uses a versioned culinary environment where each task asks for a three-ingredient portfolio from eight candidates, with Epicure scoring all 56 portfolios. The study uses anchor-cluster bootstraps and multiplicity-controlled paired tests, resolving 101 of 351 model contrasts, and the ranking replicates across independent panels and alternative metrics.

## [SPAR-Hate: Auditor-Guided Framework for Bilingual Hate Speech Parsing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22018) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SPAR-Hate introduces an auditor-guided multi-perspective role-reasoning framework for bilingual hate speech parsing, which decomposes documents into local focus units and elicits evidence-grounded candidates from Victim, Moderator, and Cultural Bystander perspectives. It resolves candidate conflicts under grounding and schema constraints, and reassembles sample-level predictions, achieving state-of-the-art results on STATE-ToxiCN and a controlled TBO split. This framework advances hate speech research from coarse-grained classification to structured parsing, enabling more precise identification of targets, supporting arguments, and target-level labels. It addresses challenges like multiple targets and culturally coded language, which are crucial for AI safety and content moderation in bilingual contexts. The framework uses separated perspective generation and arbitration, with component ablations and bounded-arbitration diagnostics confirming the contribution of each part. Structured teacher traces also support training a smaller student model, improving efficiency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Hate speech detection traditionally focuses on binary classification, but structured parsing aims to jointly identify targets, arguments, and labels. STATE-ToxiCN is the first span-level Chinese hate speech dataset, containing 8029 posts with 9533 Target-Argument-Hateful-Group quadruples. Multi-perspective reasoning, inspired by different stakeholder views, helps handle culturally coded language and conflicting interpretations.

## [SPARE: KL-Guided Context Pruning for MLLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22963) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces SPARE, a Kullback-Leibler (KL)-guided framework that prunes accumulated reasoning in multimodal tool-use agents while preserving visual evidence, achieving 37.89-64.58% token reduction with the highest average accuracy among pruning methods. This addresses the growing problem of 'textual debt' in multimodal LLM agents, where self-generated text dominates context and suppresses visual evidence, leading to inefficiency and potential errors. SPARE's favorable accuracy-context trade-off could improve the efficiency and reliability of multimodal agents in real-world applications. SPARE uses a compact task-state summary as privileged diagnostic context and replays the model under original and summary-conditioned contexts, computing reverse-KL divergence from on-policy self-distillation (OPSD) to decide pruning. The summarizer is fine-tuned with supervised fine-tuning (SFT) to enable more compact summaries and more aggressive pruning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) are increasingly used as multi-step agents that perform tasks by decomposing them and coordinating tools. During long trajectories, these agents generate extensive reasoning text that can overwhelm the context window, a phenomenon known as 'textual debt'. Pruning methods aim to remove redundant text, but must be careful not to discard crucial visual evidence. KL divergence measures the difference between probability distributions, and on-policy self-distillation (OPSD) is a technique where a model learns from its own outputs, often using privileged information.

## [Jiuge-Tuiqiao: Interactive Human-AI System for Classical Chinese Poetry Refinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23098) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Jiuge-Tuiqiao, an interactive human-AI collaborative system for classical Chinese poetry composition, which shifts from one-shot generation to a triadic model of user-driven control, ancient-guided evidence, and AI-assisted generation. Users can lock characters or lines, receive real-time prosody feedback, and obtain interpretable refinement suggestions based on high-frequency collocations, PPL-ranked classical lines, and structured knowledge from classical encyclopedias. This work addresses a culturally significant niche by enhancing human creative agency in AI-assisted poetry composition, contrasting with the passive role users often play in one-shot generation systems. Its design principles could inspire broader interactive creative tools, promoting interpretability and user engagement in human-AI collaboration. The system provides real-time prosody feedback and interpretable suggestions grounded in high-frequency collocations, PPL-ranked classical lines, and structured knowledge from classical encyclopedias. Preliminary experiments and user feedback indicate improvements in controllability, interpretability, and user engagement, though the paper is an arXiv preprint and has not been peer-reviewed.

Jiuge-Tuiqiao aims to address this by turning AI into a background assistant that supports the user's own refinement process, grounded in classical knowledge and linguistic data.

## [OODA-Tool: Separating State Tracking from Action Generation for Reliable Multi-Turn Tool Use](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24368) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces OODA-Tool, a typed closed-loop policy inspired by Boyd's OODA cycle, which separates state preservation from action realization in multi-turn tool use. It consistently improves task success across Qwen3 models (0.6B to 14B) compared to direct function-calling and ReAct policies. This addresses the state-action competition problem in LLM agents, which is critical for reliable multi-turn tool use. By decoupling state tracking from action generation, OODA-Tool offers a robust approach that could improve the reliability of AI agents in complex, multi-step tasks, especially for smaller models. OODA-Tool routes each decision through controller-checked intermediate states: Observe reconstructs task state, Orient determines execution warrant, Decide forms an admissible action structure, and Act realizes the output. The method shows larger gains on smaller models and tasks with strong dependence on accumulated information, with robustness confirmed via controlled variants, ablations, and transfer evaluations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-turn tool use in LLM agents requires maintaining an evolving task state while generating actions consistent with that state. Traditional methods like direct function-calling and ReAct couple state tracking and action generation in the same autoregressive trajectory, leading to state-action competition. The OODA loop, developed by military strategist John Boyd, is a decision-making model that emphasizes agility through iterative observe-orient-decide-act cycles, which inspired this approach.

## [Same-Player Verification in CS2 via Behavioral Fingerprints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24893) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a same-player verification method for Counter-Strike 2 that encodes a player's behavior in a match replay as a 'demo-player behavioral fingerprint' and trains a model to judge whether two observations come from the same real player. The approach achieves ROC AUCs of 0.926 on the Perfect dataset and 0.956 on the Professional dataset, with account-history AUC improving to 0.982 and 0.975 when aggregating multiple demos. This work addresses a practical problem in game analytics—detecting account sharing, boosting, and smurfing—by leveraging behavioral biometrics from gameplay traces. It provides a data-driven tool that could assist manual review processes in competitive FPS games, potentially improving integrity enforcement and fair play. The fingerprints cover five CS2-specific behavioral dimensions: crosshair control, movement-stop-fire coordination, economy/buy, combat/engagement, and temporal rhythm. Feature analysis reveals that aiming/crosshair and other low-level mechanical behaviors are the strongest identity signals, more informative than single-match performance outcomes. The study uses strict six-fold evaluations on the Perfect dataset (3,570 demos) and Professional dataset (539 demos).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: In competitive FPS games like Counter-Strike 2, account integrity reviews often need to determine if an account's recent behavior matches its historical operator, which is relevant for cases like temporary substitution, rank boosting, or smurfing. Manual review typically involves comparing a current match against multiple historical matches, which is time-consuming. This paper formalizes this as a same-player verification task, using behavioral fingerprints derived from match replays (demos) and supervised machine learning to automate the comparison. The approach leverages the rich behavioral data available in CS2 demos, which record detailed player actions.

## [CollaFuse: Split-Learning-Inspired Collaborative Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.14429) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CollaFuse introduces a novel split-learning-inspired method for collaboratively training diffusion models, reducing client computational burden while preserving privacy. Experiments on CelebA, CIFAR-10, and Animals-with-Attributes2 show enhanced performance and decreased information disclosure. This work addresses key challenges in generative AI, particularly for resource-constrained clients, by enabling collaborative diffusion model training without heavy local computation. It advances distributed machine learning and has potential applications in edge computing and privacy-sensitive domains. CollaFuse retains data and computationally inexpensive processes locally at each client while outsourcing computationally expensive processes to shared server resources. The approach is evaluated on common datasets and demonstrates improved performance while reducing the need for sharing raw data.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion models are a class of generative models that synthesize images by iteratively denoising random noise. Federated learning enables collaborative training without sharing raw data but often imposes heavy computational burdens on clients. Split learning is a privacy-preserving technique where the model is split between client and server, with the client processing initial layers locally. CollaFuse combines these ideas to reduce client burden while maintaining privacy.

The study draws on Margaret Masterman's NLP insights and applies to Orwell's political speech and Graeber's bullshit jobs.

## [LLM-QL: Enhancing Dense Retrieval with Query Likelihood Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.05216) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces LLM-QL, a method that integrates query likelihood maximization as an auxiliary task for LLM-based dense retrievers, incorporating Attention Block (AB) and Document Corruption (DC) components. Evaluations on MS MARCO and BEIR show that LLM-QL outperforms other LLM-based retrievers. This work addresses a key limitation of LLMs in dense retrieval—their lack of global information modeling—by leveraging their generative strengths. It offers a novel training paradigm that could improve retrieval performance and inspire further research in combining generative and contrastive learning for IR. LLM-QL consists of two training stages: QL learning and contrastive learning. The Attention Block (AB) prevents predictive tokens from attending to document tokens before the document's ending token, while Document Corruption (DC) masks a portion of document tokens during prediction. The method is evaluated on in-domain (MS MARCO) and out-of-domain (BEIR) datasets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Dense retrieval is a fundamental task in information retrieval, where documents and queries are encoded into dense vectors and matched based on similarity. Large language models (LLMs) have shown strong semantic understanding but are decoder-style generative models that may lack global attention. The query likelihood model is a classical IR approach that ranks documents by the probability of generating the query from a document's language model. Contrastive learning is a common technique for training dense retrievers by pulling relevant pairs together and pushing irrelevant pairs apart.

Refine-POI introduces a framework that combines topology-aware semantic ID generation using hierarchical self-organizing maps with reinforcement fine-tuning to improve next point-of-interest recommendation. It outperforms state-of-the-art baselines on three real-world datasets. This work addresses key limitations in LLM-based POI recommendation, such as topology-blind IDs and answer fixation in supervised fine-tuning. By enabling top-k list generation and better semantic representation, it could enhance the accuracy and explainability of recommendation systems. The framework uses a hierarchical self-organizing map (SOM) quantization strategy to generate semantic IDs, ensuring coordinate proximity reflects semantic similarity. It employs a policy-gradient framework to optimize top-k recommendation lists, using recommendation-driven rewards with only one ground-truth POI per example.

## [Residual Reward Models Boost Sample Efficiency in Preference-Based RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.00611) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces a Residual Reward Model (RRM) that decomposes the true reward into a prior reward and a learned residual, trained with preferences. Experiments in Meta-World and DM-Control show substantial improvements in sample efficiency over common PbRL methods, and the method was validated on a physical Franka Panda robot. This work addresses a key bottleneck in preference-based reinforcement learning (PbRL)—poor sample efficiency due to costly human feedback. By leveraging prior knowledge, RRMs could make PbRL more practical for real-world robotic applications, reducing the amount of human preference data needed. The prior reward can be an engineering heuristic, a language-generated reward, or a reward learned via inverse reinforcement learning. The method is implemented as a fork of B-Pref, reusing PEBBLE machinery, with baselines including PEBBLE, AIRL, MRN, SURF, SAC, and DrQ-v2.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Preference-based reinforcement learning (PbRL) learns reward models from human preferences, which is useful when reward design is difficult. However, PbRL often requires extensive human feedback, limiting its applicability. Prior work has used demonstrations to pre-train reward models, but transitioning between different loss functions can cause optimization instability. The Residual Reward Model addresses this by combining a prior reward with a learned residual, improving stability and sample efficiency.

## [AirLLM: Diffusion Policy-Based Adaptive LoRA for Remote LLM Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.11515) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AirLLM introduces a hierarchical diffusion policy framework that adaptively selects LoRA ranks for remote fine-tuning of LLMs over wireless links, combining PPO for coarse decisions and DDIM for fine-grained refinement. Experiments show it improves fine-tuning performance while significantly reducing transmission costs. This work addresses the practical challenge of efficiently fine-tuning LLMs on edge devices with limited bandwidth and computational resources. By making LoRA rank selection communication-aware, it enables more scalable and cost-effective remote fine-tuning, which is crucial for deploying LLMs in wireless and edge environments. The framework models rank configuration as a structured action vector spanning all LoRA-inserted projections, and uses PPO to generate coarse-grained decisions based on wireless states and linguistic complexity, refined by DDIM under Classifier-Free Guidance. Experiments under varying signal-to-noise ratios demonstrate consistent performance gains and reduced transmission costs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning method that updates only low-rank matrices, reducing computational and memory costs. However, traditional LoRA uses fixed or heuristic rank configurations, which can be inefficient for over-the-air transmission in wireless settings. AirLLM leverages reinforcement learning and diffusion models to adaptively select ranks based on channel conditions and task complexity, optimizing the trade-off between model accuracy and communication overhead.

## [MCCE: Hybrid Multi-LLM Framework for Discrete Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.06270) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces MCCE (Multi-LLM Collaborative Co-evolution), a hybrid framework that combines a frozen closed-source LLM with a lightweight trainable model for multi-objective discrete optimization. It achieves state-of-the-art Pareto front quality on multi-objective drug design benchmarks, outperforming existing baselines. This work introduces a new paradigm for hybrid LLM systems, enabling continual evolution by combining knowledge-driven exploration from closed-source models with experience-driven learning from trainable models. It could significantly impact fields like molecular design and other discrete optimization problems, offering a practical way to leverage both powerful priors and adaptive learning. MCCE maintains a trajectory memory of past search processes and uses similarity-filtered preference learning to refine the small model via reinforcement learning. Unlike model distillation, the two models mutually inspire each other, enhancing both capabilities during global exploration.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-objective discrete optimization problems, such as molecular design, involve optimizing multiple conflicting objectives over a vast combinatorial space. Traditional evolutionary algorithms often get trapped in local optima, while large language models (LLMs) offer powerful priors and reasoning ability. However, closed-source LLMs cannot be fine-tuned, and smaller open models lack broad knowledge. MCCE addresses this by combining both types of models in a collaborative co-evolution framework.

## [MENTOR: RL-Based Tool-Use Distillation with Flexible Teacher Rewards](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.18383) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces MENTOR, an on-policy distillation framework that uses a flexible teacher-optimized reward to distill tool-use capabilities from large language models (LLMs) to small language models (SLMs). It addresses the limitations of supervised fine-tuning (SFT) and sparse-reward RL by balancing behavioral alignment with downstream performance. This work is significant because it provides a more effective method for distilling complex tool-use skills into smaller models, which is crucial for efficient deployment in real-world applications. It could improve the generalization of small models to out-of-domain tasks, reducing the reliance on expensive large models. MENTOR uses a process-aware reward that leverages the teacher's reference to guide tool-use behavior, avoiding rigid trajectory replication. Experiments on controlled executable-tool benchmarks show that MENTOR improves out-of-domain tool-use performance compared to SFT and strict RL baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge distillation is a common technique to transfer capabilities from large models to smaller ones. Supervised fine-tuning (SFT) is an off-policy method that imitates teacher trajectories, but it suffers from poor out-of-domain generalization. Reinforcement learning (RL) offers an alternative, but sparse rewards provide insufficient guidance for small models. On-policy distillation trains a student on its own generated samples, which can help address distribution mismatch.

<a id="item-272"></a>
## [New Diagnostic Predicts Conformal Prediction Failures Under Distribution Shift](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.00908) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces SHAP concentration, the fraction of feature importance concentrated in the top feature, as a pre-deployment diagnostic to predict which conformal prediction models will fail under distribution shift. It is validated on COVID-19 supply chain tasks and external datasets, showing strong association with failure severity. This matters because conformal prediction guarantees degrade under distribution shift, and practitioners currently lack tools to anticipate failures before observing test data. The proposed diagnostic could enable more reliable deployment of machine learning models in critical domains like healthcare and supply chain. The diagnostic is specifically effective for gradient-boosted classifiers, identifying concentrated-dependence failures, but it does not detect global-sensitivity failures in neural networks. The paper also proves a theorem showing that Adaptive Prediction Sets conformity-score bounds worsen monotonically with concentration under explicit assumptions, and provides a decision framework with an uncertainty band around a concentration threshold.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Conformal prediction is a framework for uncertainty quantification that provides distribution-free coverage guarantees under the assumption of exchangeability. However, when distribution shift occurs, these guarantees can degrade, and existing shift detectors often cannot distinguish between catastrophic and robust outcomes. SHAP (SHapley Additive exPlanations) is a method for explaining model predictions by assigning feature importance values, typically used for post-hoc explanation. This paper repurposes SHAP as a pre-deployment diagnostic to assess model vulnerability to distribution shift.

## [SubTTA: Subspace Alignment for VLM Test-Time Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.08139) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces SubTTA, a novel method that aligns semantic subspaces of visual and textual modalities to improve test-time adaptation of vision-language models under distribution shifts. It achieves an average improvement of 2.24% over state-of-the-art TTA methods on various benchmarks. This work addresses two fundamental limitations in existing TTA methods—modality gap and visual nuisance—which often lead to unreliable pseudo-labels and misguide adaptation. By providing a principled subspace alignment approach, SubTTA could enhance the robustness of VLMs in real-world deployment where distribution shifts are common. SubTTA aligns the visual manifold to the textual semantic anchor by minimizing their chordal distance, and projects aligned visual features onto the task-specific textual subspace to filter out task-irrelevant noise. The method is evaluated on multiple benchmarks and VLM architectures, with code available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) like CLIP embed images and text into a shared space but suffer from a modality gap where image and text features cluster separately. Test-time adaptation (TTA) adapts a pre-trained model to unlabeled test data on the fly, but existing methods often rely on zero-shot predictions as pseudo-labels, which can be unreliable under distribution shifts. SubTTA addresses these issues by aligning semantic subspaces of both modalities to improve the quality of pseudo-labels and guide the adaptation process.

Researchers introduced MentorQA, the first multilingual dataset and evaluation framework for mentorship-focused question answering from long-form videos, containing nearly 9,000 QA pairs from 180 hours of content across four languages. The study compares Single-Agent, Dual-Agent, RAG, and Multi-Agent architectures, finding that Multi-Agent pipelines produce higher-quality mentorship responses, especially for complex topics and lower-resource languages. It provides a multilingual benchmark that can guide the development of educational AI systems and agentic architectures, potentially improving AI-assisted learning and career guidance. The dataset includes nearly 9,000 QA pairs generated from 180 hours of video content across four languages. The evaluation framework defines mentorship-focused dimensions beyond factual accuracy, and the study also analyzes the reliability of automated LLM-based evaluation, finding substantial variation in alignment with human judgments.

SynthCharge is a new parametric generator that creates diverse, feasibility-screened instances for the Electric Vehicle Routing Problem with Time Windows (EVRPTW), supporting up to 500 customers with experiments on 5 to 100. It integrates instance geometry, adaptive energy capacity scaling, and range-aware charging station placement, and filters out unsolvable instances via fast feasibility screening. This addresses the lack of reproducible and verifiable benchmarks for learning-based EVRPTW solvers, enabling systematic evaluation of neural routing and data-driven approaches. It provides dynamic benchmarking infrastructure that can accelerate research in sustainable logistics and optimization. The generator currently focuses on instances with 5 to 100 customers, though it can scale to 500. Feasibility screening ensures structural validity by removing unsolvable instances, and the generator supports varying spatiotemporal configurations.

## [ZipCal: Fast Model-Agnostic Data Curation for LLM Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.16105) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces ZipCal, a model-agnostic data curation strategy that uses Zipfian power laws to select calibration data for pruning and quantization of large language models. It outperforms uniform random sampling and matches a state-of-the-art perplexity-based method while being about 240 times faster. This work addresses an underexplored but critical aspect of LLM compression: calibration data selection. By offering a fast, model-agnostic alternative to expensive perplexity-based methods, ZipCal can make compression more practical for large-scale models and datasets, potentially lowering deployment costs. ZipCal maximizes lexical diversity based on Zipfian power laws, which describe the rank-frequency distribution of words in natural language. The method has linear complexity, making it tractable for large datasets, and the code is publicly available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Post-training compression techniques like pruning and quantization are essential for deploying large language models on resource-constrained devices. These methods typically require a small set of calibration data to determine optimal compression configurations, but the choice of this data is often overlooked. Zipf's law is a statistical distribution observed in natural language where the frequency of a word is inversely proportional to its rank, and it can be used to characterize lexical diversity.

## [VeriFi: Unified Watermarking for Face Recovery and Tamper Localization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.23940) ⭐️ 7.0/10

VeriFi is a novel versatile watermarking framework that unifies copyright protection, pixel-level manipulation localization, and high-fidelity face content recovery using a compact semantic latent watermark. It outperforms state-of-the-art baselines on CelebA-HQ and FFHQ in robustness, localization accuracy, and recovery quality. This work addresses the fidelity-functionality trade-off in existing versatile watermarking systems and adds content recovery capability, which is crucial for deepfake forensics and media provenance. It provides a practical defense against AIGC-driven face manipulation, benefiting media integrity and copyright protection. VeriFi embeds a compact semantic latent watermark that serves as a content-preserving prior, enabling faithful restoration even after severe manipulations. It achieves fine-grained localization without dedicated payloads by correlating image features with decoded provenance signals, and introduces an AIGC attack simulator combining latent-space mixing with seamless blending for enhanced robustness.

**Background**: Versatile watermarking systems embed information into images for copyright protection and tamper localization, but often face a trade-off between visual quality and functionality. Traditional methods rely on explicit localization payloads, which degrade quality and robustness. Semantic latent watermarks, embedded in latent spaces, offer a promising alternative by preserving content while enabling recovery. This paper builds on these concepts to unify multiple forensic tasks.

## [Unified Conditional Flow for Motion Generation, Editing, and Retargeting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.13427) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a unified conditional-flow framework that integrates text-driven motion generation, semantic editing, and intra-structural retargeting into a single rectified-flow model, eliminating the need for separate pipelines. The model, a text- and skeleton-conditioned rectified-flow transformer, supports zero-shot editing and retargeting without task-specific fine-tuning. This unified approach simplifies the motion synthesis pipeline, reducing complexity and potential errors from incompatible representations. It could significantly impact 3D animation and character control by enabling a single model to handle multiple tasks, improving efficiency and consistency. The model uses per-joint tokenization and explicit joint self-attention to capture spatial kinematic dependencies, and injects text conditions at both joint and frame levels. Residual multi-condition classifier-free guidance balances text adherence and skeletal conformity, and experiments on SnapMoGen and a multi-character Mixamo subset demonstrate the framework's effectiveness.

Intra-structural retargeting adjusts motion to skeletons with the same topology but different bone lengths or rest poses, traditionally handled as geometric post-processing.

## [MOMO: Multimodal Framework for Robot Skill Learning and Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.20468) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MOMO is a new interactive framework that enables non-expert users to adapt robot skills through three complementary modalities: kinesthetic touch, natural language, and a graphical web interface. It integrates a tool-based LLM architecture that selects predefined functions for safe adaptation, and was validated on a 7-DoF robot at the Automatica 2025 trade fair. This framework addresses the growing need for flexible robot systems that non-experts can easily adapt in industrial settings, potentially lowering the barrier to robot programming. By combining multiple interaction modalities and an LLM-based tool architecture, it demonstrates a practical path toward more intuitive and safe human-robot collaboration. The framework comprises five components: energy-based human-intention detection, a tool-based LLM architecture (which parameterizes predefined functions rather than generating code), Kernelized Movement Primitives (KMPs) for motion encoding, probabilistic Virtual Fixtures for guided demonstration recording, and ergodic control for surface finishing. The tool-based LLM architecture generalizes skill adaptation from KMPs to ergodic control, enabling voice-commanded surface finishing.

Probabilistic Virtual Fixtures enable adaptive haptic feedback based on learned uncertainty, keeping the human in the loop for precision tasks.

## [MambaCSP: Hybrid-Attention SSM for Hardware-Efficient Channel State Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.21957) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MambaCSP introduces a hybrid-attention state space model (SSM) that replaces LLM-based backbones with a linear-time Mamba model for channel state prediction (CSP). It incorporates lightweight patch-mixer attention layers to overcome the local-only dependency of pure SSMs, achieving 9-12% higher prediction accuracy than LLM-based approaches while delivering up to 3.0x higher throughput, 2.6x lower VRAM usage, and 2.9x faster inference. This work addresses the quadratic scaling and high resource consumption of attention-based models in wireless communications, offering a scalable and hardware-efficient alternative for real-time and resource-constrained deployments. It highlights the potential of hybrid state space architectures for AI-native CSI prediction in future wireless networks, which could accelerate the adoption of AI in 5G/6G systems. The MambaCSP model is evaluated on MISO-OFDM simulations, showing consistent improvements over LLM-based baselines. The patch-mixer attention layers are periodically injected to capture cross-token dependencies, balancing long-context prediction with computational efficiency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Channel state information (CSI) describes how a signal propagates from transmitter to receiver, and accurate prediction is crucial for future wireless systems. Traditional attention-based models like transformers and LLMs can capture long-range dependencies but suffer from quadratic scaling in sequence length, making them inefficient for real-time applications. Selective state space models (SSMs) like Mamba offer linear-time complexity and hardware efficiency, but they have limited ability to model long-range dependencies. MambaCSP combines SSMs with lightweight attention to address this limitation.

A new study applies AICON, a reactive gradient-descent framework from robotics, to the Tower of London test, showing it can predict human planning difficulty across 24 problems better than structural baselines, especially for groups with reduced planning capacity. The model generalizes to held-out problems in a leave-two-out evaluation, and its failure modes resemble those of Parkinson's patients who struggle with goal hierarchies but not move counts.

## [MIMO: A Two-Stage Framework for Multilingual Information Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.31171) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MIMO proposes a two-stage training framework for multilingual information retrieval (MLIR) that anchors to a stable English semantic space from a high-performing teacher model. It first initializes cross-lingual alignment via knowledge distillation, then jointly optimizes distillation and cross-lingual contrastive learning to improve retrieval discrimination while preserving alignment. This framework addresses a critical gap in MLIR, where existing embedding models often degrade in mixed-language corpora. By mitigating language clustering and balancing alignment and uniformity, MIMO could improve real-world multilingual search systems and set a new benchmark for cross-lingual retrieval. MIMO consistently outperforms existing cross-lingual training baselines across various MLIR and Multi-Monolingual benchmarks, and remains competitive with off-the-shelf models of similar or larger parameter scales. The paper also provides an Alignment-Uniformity analysis clarifying the distinct roles of the two loss components and their favorable trade-off.

**Background**: Multilingual information retrieval (MLIR) involves searching across documents in multiple languages, where queries and documents may be in different languages. Traditional embedding models are often optimized for multi-monolingual retrieval and struggle with cross-lingual scenarios. Conventional contrastive learning can exacerbate language clustering, leading to a trade-off between cross-lingual alignment and embedding uniformity. MIMO leverages knowledge distillation from a strong English teacher model to anchor the semantic space, aiming to improve cross-lingual alignment without sacrificing retrieval performance.

## [SHIFT: Training-Free Method to Correct Language Bias in Multilingual Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18801) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SHIFT introduces a training-free, index-side feature transformation that estimates and subtracts language-specific offsets from document embeddings using parallel translation pairs, effectively mitigating language bias in multilingual dense retrieval. This addresses a critical issue in multilingual information retrieval where models favor same-language documents, potentially hiding more relevant cross-lingual results. By offering a lightweight, training-free solution, SHIFT could improve the fairness and effectiveness of multilingual search systems without requiring retraining. SHIFT operates at the indexing stage, using parallel translation pairs to compute a relative language vector for each target language relative to a source language, then subtracts this vector from document embeddings. The method was evaluated on four MLIR benchmarks across diverse dense retrieval models, showing consistent improvements in mitigating language bias.

**Background**: Multilingual dense retrieval encodes queries and documents into a shared embedding space using multilingual transformers like mBERT, enabling semantic matching across languages. However, these models often exhibit language bias, favoring documents in the same language as the query, which can degrade cross-lingual retrieval performance. Feature transformation techniques modify input features to improve model performance, and SHIFT applies this concept at the index side to correct language-specific offsets.

## [PPE-Bench: New Benchmark for MLLM Unlearning with Private-Public Entanglement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02897) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PPE-Bench is a new benchmark introduced to evaluate machine unlearning in Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) under private-public entanglement, where each image contains a target individual to be forgotten alongside public information such as public figures or landmarks. The benchmark addresses limitations of existing benchmarks by using realistic, complex images and considering the entanglement of private and public information. This benchmark is significant because it addresses a critical gap in MLLM unlearning evaluation, reflecting real-world visual complexity and the entanglement of private and public data. It provides a more realistic testbed for developing unlearning methods that can remove private information without damaging adjacent public knowledge, which is crucial for privacy protection and AI safety. The benchmark includes images with a target individual to be forgotten and public information to be preserved, such as public figures and landmarks. The paper also introduces two simple but effective methods to better preserve public information during unlearning, and experiments show that existing unlearning methods can reduce private information leakage but often substantially harm adjacent public information.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) can memorize private information from web data, raising privacy concerns. Machine unlearning offers a way to remove such private knowledge without retraining from scratch. However, existing MLLM unlearning benchmarks have limitations: they rely on simplified images with only the target individual, and they assume the forget set and retain set are fully separated, ignoring real-world visual entanglement. PPE-Bench addresses these issues by incorporating realistic images and private-public entanglement.

## [REPREC: Lightweight LLM-Based Sequential Recommendation via Frozen Encoder Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24845) ⭐️ 7.0/10

REPREC introduces a lightweight framework that aligns a frozen sequential encoder's user embedding with a frozen LLM via a small MLP injector, enabling efficient LLM-based sequential recommendation without fine-tuning. The framework trains only the injector, leaving both pretrained backbones unchanged, and consistently outperforms LoRA on multiple benchmark datasets. This approach significantly reduces training complexity and deployment cost for LLM-based recommendation systems, making them more accessible for production use. It also demonstrates that parameter-efficient methods can achieve competitive performance, potentially accelerating adoption of LLMs in recommendation tasks. REPREC maps a fixed-size user embedding into a small set of learned soft tokens via the MLP injector, conditioning the frozen LLM. When trained on short prompt histories and evaluated with longer contexts, it maintains 85-100% of LoRA's performance while reducing per-epoch training time by an average of 1.51X.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) have been applied to sequential recommendation by formulating it as a natural language task. Previous work often relied on LLM fine-tuning or additional architectural modules, which increased training complexity and deployment cost. REPREC avoids these by using a frozen sequential encoder and a frozen LLM, with only a small MLP injector trained to align the user embedding with the LLM's input space.

## [UniVVT: Unified End-to-End Video Virtual Try-On Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05745) ⭐️ 7.0/10

UniVVT introduces a unified end-to-end framework for video virtual try-on that replaces multi-stage modules with a multimodal LLM-based perceiver and a semantic bridge, eliminating the need for explicit masks, poses, and warping at inference. The paper reports state-of-the-art performance across multiple benchmarks. This approach addresses the error propagation problem in multi-stage virtual try-on systems, potentially simplifying deployment and improving robustness. It could influence future research by demonstrating that implicit semantic guidance can replace fragile geometric preprocessing in video generation tasks. The framework uses a three-stage progressive training strategy: semantic alignment, joint task adaptation, and flexible-resolution refinement. The scene-task perceiver encodes source video, target garment, and task instruction into compact latent tokens, which are then aligned with a diffusion-based video generator via a lightweight semantic bridge.

Traditional approaches rely on mask-conditioned video inpainting with separate modules for human parsing, pose estimation, and garment warping, which can be error-prone and hard to deploy. Multimodal large language models (LLMs) can encode diverse inputs into a unified representation, and semantic bridges help align these representations with generative models.

## [ScaleSense: Learned Resource Estimation for Cost-Efficient Scaling in AnalyticDB](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07945) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ScaleSense is a proactive, query-level resource scaling framework for Alibaba AnalyticDB that uses a multi-faceted query encoder and a quantile-based resource predictor to estimate multi-dimensional physical footprints, enabling an auto-scaling controller to navigate the performance-cost Pareto frontier without model retraining. Evaluations on over 1.36 million production queries show a 76.7% relative improvement in optimal resource configuration selection over the best baseline, reducing monetary cost by up to 5.22x under performance-optimization policy. This framework addresses the costly 'provisioning trap' in serverless data warehouses, where users over-provision resources due to fear of resource depletion, wasting budgets without solving non-CPU bottlenecks like I/O saturation. By providing accurate resource estimation and cost-aware scaling, ScaleSense can significantly reduce operational costs and improve resource efficiency for cloud data warehouse users, potentially influencing future resource management systems in the industry. ScaleSense's query encoder jointly models plan topologies and hardware specifications, and the quantile-based predictor provides prediction intervals as a safety net. The auto-scaling controller dynamically tailors allocations to business priorities without retraining, and the system achieves state-of-the-art prediction accuracy with low-overhead inference latency, confirming practical performance in production deployments.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Cloud-native serverless data warehouses decouple storage and compute to achieve fine-grained elasticity, but determining optimal resource allocation for heterogeneous ad-hoc queries is challenging. Alibaba AnalyticDB is a real-time, cloud-native data warehouse that uses an MPP architecture, and it faces the 'provisioning trap' where users over-provision to avoid catastrophic resource depletion. ScaleSense uses machine learning to estimate resource footprints and guide scaling decisions, addressing this industrial challenge.

## [AD2-Bench: Benchmark and EGVOR Framework for Trustworthy Multimodal Reasoning in Complex Urban Scenes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10954) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces AD2-Bench, a new benchmark with a Hierarchical Visual Diagnosis framework that decomposes multimodal reasoning into a Chain of Evidence (CoE) to diagnose reasoning failures in complex urban scenes. It also proposes Evidence-grounded Visual Reasoning (EGVOR), which uses structured spatial-semantic triplets (Evidence Atoms) to improve reasoning stability under adverse conditions. This work addresses a critical gap in existing outcome-oriented benchmarks by focusing on diagnosing the reasoning process itself, not just final predictions. It provides a framework for building more trustworthy multimodal AI systems, which is essential for safety-critical applications like autonomous driving and urban surveillance. The Hierarchical Visual Diagnosis framework identifies two primary causes of reasoning failure: Spatial Ambiguity (localization errors) and Semantic Uncertainty (understanding errors). EGVOR is trained via a hierarchical curriculum that progresses from reflective supervision construction to reinforcement learning, explicitly rewarding reduced reasoning variance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) combine vision and language understanding, but their performance degrades in complex scenes with adverse conditions. Existing benchmarks often only evaluate final answers, missing failures in the reasoning process. The Chain of Evidence (CoE) approach, as seen in related work, guides models to generate structured evidence descriptions, improving localization and reasoning.

## [REOPD: Adaptive Reward Extrapolation to Prevent Reward Hacking in On-Policy Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11698) ⭐️ 7.0/10

REOPD introduces a reliability-adaptive reward extrapolation framework for on-policy distillation, which adjusts token-level coefficients to prevent reward hacking without requiring extra models or rollouts. It outperforms G-OPD on single-teacher mathematics and both domains in the multi-teacher setting, while matching G-OPD on single-teacher code. This work addresses a critical problem in on-policy distillation—reward hacking—which can destabilize training and degrade model performance. By providing a fine-grained, adaptive approach, REOPD could improve the efficiency and reliability of LLM training, making distillation more practical for diverse domains. REOPD combines a token-level compatibility weight with a batch-level adaptive budget, yielding a token-wise coefficient λ_b,t = 1 + γ_b q_t. This allows selective extrapolation along reliable teacher-reference directions while preserving teacher alignment, and requires no verifier, reward model, value model, or extra rollout beyond standard OPD.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: On-policy distillation (OPD) trains a student on its own trajectories under dense token-level supervision from a teacher. Reward-extrapolation methods like ExOPD amplify the teacher-reference log-likelihood ratio to go beyond direct imitation, but they apply a single global coefficient to all tokens, which can cause reward hacking and unstable training. REOPD aims to fix this by adapting the coefficient per token based on reliability.

## [12-CNOT Double Qubit Excitation Gate Achieves Record Efficiency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11733) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper presents the first reported 12-CNOT decomposition of the double qubit excitation operator, reducing CNOT count from 13 to 12 and CNOT depth from 11 to 8 (27% reduction) compared to previous state-of-the-art circuits. With output qubit relabeling, the CNOT depth can be further reduced to 7 (36% reduction). This improvement is significant because the double qubit excitation operator is used as a building block hundreds or thousands of times in practical quantum algorithms, such as UCCSD in VQE. Even small reductions in CNOT count and depth compound across the entire circuit, leading to substantial resource savings and improved feasibility of near-term quantum computing. The new circuit achieves the lowest CNOT count (12), lowest CNOT depth (8), and lowest total circuit depth (15) among all previous state-of-the-art circuits, while adding only 2 extra single-qubit gates (from 11 to 13). The decomposition is compared against previous 13-CNOT circuits across four different metrics.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: The double qubit excitation operator is a quantum gate that implements a fermionic excitation between two qubits, commonly used in quantum chemistry simulations and variational algorithms like UCCSD. CNOT gates are a primary source of error and overhead in quantum circuits, so minimizing their count and depth is crucial for practical quantum computing. This work contributes to ongoing efforts in quantum circuit optimization, which aim to make complex algorithms executable on near-term quantum hardware.

## [M-Net: Integrating Spectral Features and Physical Field Operators into Deep Learning for Medical Image Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12196) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes M-Net, a U-Net variant that integrates matrix spectral features (condition number) and physical field operators (divergence and curl-like boundary irregularity) as mathematical inductive biases, achieving Dice scores of 78.42%, 76.15%, and 83.67% on LiTS, KiTS, and BraTS benchmarks, outperforming baseline U-Net by 12.37%, 3.52%, and 5.55% respectively. This work demonstrates that explicit mathematical priors can complement data-driven learning in medical image segmentation, potentially improving accuracy in clinical applications. It opens new avenues for integrating linear algebra and vector calculus into deep architectures, which could benefit other imaging tasks. The M-Net integrates three components: continuous spectral features from condition numbers, physical field operators (divergence and a discrete curl-like operator), and a Math-Attention Gate (MAG) that fuses mathematical features with CNN features at skip connections. Ablations show the condition-number feature adds 2.14% over binary invertibility features, and MAG adds 1.45% over simple concatenation.

**Background**: Deep learning models like U-Net are widely used for medical image segmentation but often ignore the mathematical structure inherent in images. Inductive biases are assumptions that help algorithms learn patterns; integrating mathematical priors such as spectral analysis and vector calculus can provide complementary information. The condition number measures ill-conditioning of local pixel matrices, while divergence and curl-like operators capture intensity extrema and edge irregularities.

The paper introduces ARMDIL, an adaptive router that uses a multimodal large language model (MLLM) to dynamically select the most suitable vision backbone for each image from a heterogeneous ensemble of ResNet, SSL, and VLM models. This approach improves robustness and adaptability in cross-dataset image classification, allowing new information to be integrated via simple prompt modifications. This work addresses a critical challenge in domain generalization, where models trained on single datasets often fail to generalize across domains. By leveraging an MLLM router, ARMDIL offers a flexible and interpretable solution that could enhance the reliability of general-purpose vision systems like AI assistants and autonomous robots. ARMDIL employs an ensemble of convolutional neural networks (ResNets), self-supervised representation learners (SSL), and vision language models (VLMs), all trained on a unified label space constructed from multiple datasets. Empirical evaluations show that ARMDIL performs competitively with specialized training-based routers while offering improved adaptability and interpretability through natural language reasoning traces.

## [X2Localizer: Cross-grained Alignment for Progressive Cross-view Video Geo-localization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16658) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Progressive Cross-view Video Geo-localization (PCVG), a new evaluation protocol for deployment-oriented localization, and proposes X2Localizer, a cross-grained alignment framework with a Sliding-Window Re-Localization (SWRL) strategy. X2Localizer improves early localization and achieves state-of-the-art results in single-frame settings, with gains of +4.7 Recall@1 and +11.5 Recall@10 over previous methods. This work addresses practical limitations of existing cross-view video geo-localization methods, which rely on fixed-length inputs and post-hoc refinement, hindering real-time deployment. By enabling progressive localization under varying temporal budgets and partial observations, it narrows the gap between benchmark evaluation and real-world applications, potentially benefiting autonomous driving, robotics, and augmented reality. X2Localizer jointly supervises global prefix-to-aerial retrieval and token-aggregated frame–aerial-tile matching with a budget-dependent asymmetric objective. The SWRL strategy dynamically refreshes candidate regions for failure recovery and long-range deployment without full-sequence reprocessing, and experiments show it preserves conventional full-video performance with marginal gains.

## [Complexity Induction: Compositional Generalization via Structured Training Distortion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21464) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces 'complexity induction', a method that applies structured distortion to training data to achieve compositional generalization in standard CNNs without architectural changes. Demonstrated on synthetic images of colored shapes, it enables prediction of unseen color-shape combinations. This work challenges the notion that compositional generalization requires architectural modifications, potentially simplifying how models can be trained to handle novel combinations. It may influence future training strategies in vision and language models, and offers insights into the role of structured data distortion in learning. Two distortion methods based on Jaccard string similarity are used: mixed labels (soft targets) and expanded dataset (false samples with structurally motivated labels). A control with random false labels confirms that the effect depends on structure, not noise.

**Background**: Compositional generalization is the ability to understand novel combinations of known concepts, a key challenge in AI. Standard neural networks often fail at this when trained on limited data. This paper explores whether structured distortion of training data can induce this ability without architectural changes, drawing parallels to how natural language might aid cognitive development.

## [CAT-GS: A Backpropagation Controller for Stable Multimodal Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24947) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CAT-GS introduces a backpropagation-based optimization controller that addresses modality imbalance, unstable gating, and fusion interference in multimodal neural networks without modifying model architectures. It was evaluated on benchmarks like CREMA-D, AV-MNIST, and VGGSound, showing improved or matched accuracy against strong baselines. This work addresses a critical challenge in multimodal learning, which is increasingly important in applications like autonomous driving and multimedia analysis. By providing a training-time solution that works across architectures, CAT-GS could improve the robustness and performance of many multimodal systems without requiring redesign. CAT-GS uses temperature scaling and EMA smoothing to calibrate teacher-derived reliability, a margin-thresholded policy to switch between warm-up dropout, weak-modality prioritization, and weak-biased blending, and capped gradient-budget renormalization to stabilize gradients. It also applies fusion-only PCGrad to reduce cross-modal interference at the shared fusion layer.

**Background**: Multimodal learning integrates multiple data types like audio, visual, and text, but often suffers from modality imbalance where one modality dominates, leading to underutilization of others. Gating mechanisms control information flow in neural networks, but can be unstable. Fusion layers combine modality-specific features, but gradient conflicts can arise. CAT-GS targets these issues during backpropagation.

## [MACGen: Multi-Agent Framework for Secure Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.25457) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MACGen is a new multi-agent framework that integrates planning, security analysis, code synthesis, and refinement to jointly optimize functional correctness and security in LLM-generated code. It improves F&S@1 over direct prompting by 19.61 and 10.57 percentage points on CWEval and BaxBench, respectively. This framework addresses a critical challenge in LLM-based code generation: producing code that is both functionally correct and secure. By separating agent roles and using structured artifacts, it reduces context bloat and improves security, which could lead to more reliable and secure AI-assisted software development. MACGen uses a planner, security advisor, coder, and reviewer, each receiving only structured artifacts from upstream stages rather than full dialogue histories. This enforces role specialization and reduces uncontrolled context growth. The framework was evaluated on CWEval and BaxBench benchmarks, showing significant improvements in functional and security correctness.

Secure code generation is a multi-objective problem requiring both functional correctness and security. Existing approaches use external security knowledge or agentic feedback, but they often suffer from context bloat or fail to translate generic advice into task-specific implementations. MACGen addresses these issues by using a multi-agent collaboration with structured artifacts.

## [Stale Constraints in Inherited Agent Memory Undermine Verification Budgets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.25553) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a model of supersession in agent memory, showing that under a fixed verification budget of two records, agents fail to detect superseded constraints in most episodes, leading to stale-consistent decisions in 77.3%, 74.7%, and 74.7% of cases across runs. Reallocating one verification slot to the critical provenance path improves current-record-consistent decisions by up to 74.0 percentage points. This research highlights a critical safety gap in autonomous agents that inherit consolidated memory: stale constraints can lead to incorrect decisions even when verification is performed. The findings inform the design of verification policies and memory systems, suggesting that freshness or supersession signals are needed beyond simple relevance. The study uses a fixed verification budget of two records, comparing native allocation with reallocating one slot to the critical provenance path or a random record. The intervention is not a scheduler but quantifies the reduction in stale-consistent decisions, approaching the native missed-path rate in primary, replication, and corrected held-out runs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Agents with inherited memory may hold constraints that were true when written but have since been superseded by newer authoritative records. Verification budgets are limited, so agents must decide which records to check. Provenance tracks the origin and history of data, and the critical provenance path is the chain of records leading to the current authoritative status. The paper models supersession explicitly, treating provenance as immutable while the current record changes.

<a id="item-299"></a>
## [QLoRA Rank Tunes Fact Acquisition vs. Retention Trade-off](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.25677) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces an acquisition-retention frontier for QLoRA, showing that adapter rank controls the trade-off between learning new facts and preserving unrelated capabilities. Using a controlled OpenStreetMap-derived benchmark with Qwen3-4B, they compare full fine-tuning (FFT) with QLoRA at ranks 8, 16, 32, and 64. This finding is significant for practitioners fine-tuning large language models, as it clarifies that parameter-efficient methods like QLoRA do not automatically preserve pretrained capabilities; the choice of rank directly impacts the balance between acquiring new knowledge and avoiding catastrophic forgetting. It provides practical guidance for selecting adapter rank based on the desired trade-off. The study uses a controlled benchmark derived from OpenStreetMap, where Qwen3-4B must acquire anonymized geographic associations while retaining unrelated capabilities. They find that low-rank QLoRA preserves out-of-domain performance but acquires fewer facts, while higher ranks improve paraphrase generalization at the cost of performance on unrelated benchmarks. FFT acts as a conservative baseline, retaining general capabilities well but not reaching the highest factual-acquisition regime. A separate math adaptation experiment shows a weaker frontier, suggesting the effect is most pronounced when installing new factual associations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: QLoRA (Quantized Low-Rank Adaptation) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning method that combines 4-bit quantization with low-rank adapters, enabling fine-tuning of large models on a single GPU. It is widely used for adapting open-weight models like LLaMA. The acquisition-retention frontier is a concept introduced in this paper to describe the trade-off between learning new facts and retaining existing capabilities, which is related to catastrophic forgetting in neural networks.

## [Agentic Pipeline Reduces Outcome Leakage in ICU Mortality Explanations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26109) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A feasibility study on the eICU Demo dataset compared a standalone LLM with a four-step agentic pipeline for explaining ICU mortality predictions, finding that the agentic approach produced no explicit outcome leakage versus one case for the standalone LLM, while improving guideline grounding and value specificity. This study highlights the potential of agentic pipelines to enhance safety and clinical relevance in AI-driven explanations for high-stakes healthcare decisions, addressing a critical gap where traditional feature-attribution methods fall short. The study used 2,353 ICU stays with 8.1% mortality; XGBoost achieved AUROC 0.855 and AUPRC 0.332. On a 38-case subset, the agentic pipeline showed higher guideline grounding (0.762 vs 0.143) and value specificity (0.236 vs 0.143), but lower SHAP alignment (Jaccard 0.077 vs 0.171) and direction consistency (78.6% vs 92.9%).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: ICU mortality prediction models often rely on feature-attribution methods like SHAP, which provide numerical importance scores but lack clinical narrative. Large language models (LLMs) can generate natural language explanations, and agentic pipelines decompose the task into steps such as data interpretation, guideline checking, and final explanation. The eICU Collaborative Research Database is a multi-center critical care database, and its demo version is used for feasibility studies. Outcome leakage occurs when information not available at prediction time influences model training or explanations, leading to misleading results.

## [LLM Literature Reviews Need Human Oversight, Study Finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26145) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new study evaluated 20 AI-generated literature reviews produced by LLMs using short and long context windows, assessing them across 15 dimensions. The findings show that while LLMs can create foundational overviews, they require human refinement to meet academic standards. This research highlights the current limitations of LLMs in academic writing, emphasizing that human expertise remains essential for producing high-quality literature reviews. It informs researchers and practitioners about the appropriate use of AI in scholarly workflows. The study used sources from Semantic Scholar and Arxiv, and two researchers evaluated the reviews. Longer context windows allowed LLMs to incorporate broader information but also exacerbated issues like content repetition, omission of critical work, and a tendency toward description over synthesis.
