## [ChemOntoRule: A Verifiable Symbolic Core for AI Chemistry Problem Solving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26164) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces ChemOntoRule, a proof-of-concept symbolic core that combines a task-centric ontology with deterministic Python rules for school-level chemistry problem solving. It achieved 98.67% accuracy on 300 human-authored problems, with the ontology-driven subset covering 269 problems and matching 98.88% of references. This work addresses the critical issue of verifiability in AI-assisted chemistry problem solving, offering an alternative to opaque LLM reasoning. It demonstrates a hybrid approach where LLMs act as translators rather than primary reasoners, potentially improving trust and reliability in educational AI tools. The ontology is serialized in JSON and RDF/Turtle, and the system includes an expert-coded fallback for problem families not covered by general rules. The authors note that the evaluation measures coverage and internal consistency, not independent generalization, and they analyze the four mismatches to distinguish structural validation from chemical correctness.

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) can interpret natural-language chemistry questions but their reasoning is hard to inspect and validate. Task-centric ontology engineering focuses on concepts and procedures needed for a specific set of problems, rather than a universal representation. This paper explores combining these approaches for chemistry education.

This work demonstrates a practical approach to ontology learning that mitigates LLM hallucination and format inconsistency, which are key challenges in knowledge engineering. ## [JPGFN: Feature-Enhanced Jacobi Polynomial Filtering for Graph Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27144) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces JPGFN, a novel graph anomaly detection method that combines a Feature Separation Transformation Network (FSTNN) with an adaptive Jacobi polynomial graph filtering module and a node label constraint module. It addresses three key challenges in existing frequency-domain filtering approaches: static filters, ignored attribute importance, and underutilized labels. JPGFN advances graph anomaly detection by enabling adaptive frequency filtering and better feature learning, potentially improving detection accuracy in real-world applications like fraud detection and network monitoring. It also contributes to the broader trend of designing flexible spectral filters for graph neural networks. The adaptive Jacobi polynomial filter uses parameterized polynomials to adapt to different frequency distributions, avoiding eigendecomposition by applying polynomial operations on the normalized Laplacian. For filter order up to K=4, JPGFN evaluates Jacobi basis functions on one minus the Laplacian spectrum and fuses outputs with feature-specific learnable coefficients.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Graph anomaly detection (GAD) aims to identify unusual nodes in graph-structured data, often using graph neural networks (GNNs) that operate in the spectral domain. Frequency-domain filtering applies a filter to the graph signal's spectrum, and polynomial approximations like Jacobi polynomials allow efficient filtering without costly eigendecomposition. Existing methods often use static filters, which may not adapt to varying data distributions, and may overlook fine-grained feature information.

The paper introduces TADP, a task-aware deformable prediction method for single-stage 3D object detection, which includes a triple feature refinement aggregation module, a multi-scale feature aggregation block, and a plug-and-play task-aware deformation head. This work addresses a fundamental limitation in single-stage 3D detectors that use shared features for multiple tasks, potentially improving accuracy in autonomous driving applications. The plug-and-play design of the deformation head allows easy integration into existing detectors, which could benefit the broader 3D detection community. The method designs three different deformation modules for the task-aware deformation head, and experiments show that the deformation head generalizes well to other detection methods. The reported car mAP of 80.91% on KITTI indicates strong performance, though the paper is not yet peer-reviewed.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

## [eBay's Two-Stage XGBoost Model Boosts GMB via Counterfactual Signal Recommendation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27366) ⭐️ 6.0/10

eBay presents a two-stage XGBoost-based model that optimally populates the View-Item page with contextual signals, achieving a 0.08% lift in overall Gross Merchandise Bought (GMB) and a 0.58% increase in Parts and Accessories GMB in online experiments. This work demonstrates a practical application of counterfactual estimation and gradient boosting in e-commerce, showing that even small, targeted improvements in signal recommendation can yield measurable business impact. It highlights the value of data-driven optimization in real-world recommendation systems. The model uses a two-stage XGBoost architecture, likely first predicting the probability of purchase or engagement, then selecting the optimal set of signals. The reported lifts are modest, and the paper appears to be an industry report rather than a major research breakthrough, with no community comments provided.

<a id="item-313"></a>
## [Study Finds Limited Recording-Specific Recoverability from Contact PPG to Camera rPPG](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27392) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This study evaluated property-specific recoverability from contact PPG to camera rPPG using 655 recordings from the MMPD dataset with the CHROM algorithm, finding that while population-level correlations exist, recording-specific correspondence is limited, with maximal Lyapunov exponents showing no significant correlation. This research addresses a critical gap in rPPG validation by moving beyond endpoint accuracy to examine whether physiological properties are preserved per recording, which is essential for clinical and research applications that rely on individual-level accuracy. The largely negative findings highlight the need for more robust rPPG methods and careful interpretation of rPPG-derived metrics. The study used CHROM as a fixed camera-rPPG pathway, reproducing a heart-rate MAE of 15.26 bpm and Pearson correlation of 0.0801. Maximal Lyapunov exponents showed essentially no recording-specific correspondence (correlation 0.0231, p=0.5584), and endpoint discrepancy exhibited Fitzpatrick-associated heterogeneity, with a Fitzpatrick VI vs III contrast of 9.32 bpm.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Remote photoplethysmography (rPPG) estimates physiological signals like heart rate from camera videos, while contact photoplethysmography (PPG) uses sensors on the skin. The CHROM algorithm is a chrominance-based rPPG method that extracts pulse rate from color changes in skin. The Multi-Domain Mobile Video Physiology Dataset (MMPD) contains 11 hours of mobile phone recordings from 33 subjects, providing a diverse testbed for rPPG validation. Maximal Lyapunov exponents measure the predictability of dynamical systems, and here they are used to assess whether the underlying dynamics of PPG are preserved in rPPG.

## [Four Integer Programming Models for Cellular Manufacturing with Alternative Process Plans](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.15361) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces four integer programming formulations that simultaneously optimize cell formation and operational routing in cellular manufacturing systems (CMS), accommodating multiple process plans per part and multiple machines per operation. Unlike prior sequential approaches, these models integrate machine flexibility and alternative process plans into a unified objective. This unified approach can lead to more globally optimal configurations for generalized grouping problems, potentially reducing inter-cell and intra-cell movements and improving manufacturing efficiency. It addresses a known limitation in CMS design, where cell formation and routing are often treated separately, and could benefit production planning and operational decision-making in flexible manufacturing environments. The models minimize inter-cell and intra-cell movements by assigning consecutive operations of a part to the same cell and machine, and this objective is compared against alternatives like reducing machine investment and operating costs. Numerical examples demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed formulations in practice.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Cellular manufacturing is a production strategy that groups machines into cells to process part families, aiming to reduce setup times, work-in-process inventory, and material handling. The design of CMS involves cell formation, which is the grouping of parts and machines, and operational routing, which determines the sequence of operations and machine assignments. Integer programming has been widely used to model cell formation problems, but traditional models often simplify assumptions and treat design and operational decisions sequentially. Alternative process plans provide flexibility in manufacturing by allowing a part to be produced through different sequences of operations, which can improve resource utilization and scheduling.

<a id="item-315"></a>
## [LLM Evaluator Reliability vs. Expert Alignment in Japanese Counseling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02950) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A fixed-benchmark study compared four LLM evaluators (GPT-5.5, Gemini 3.5 Flash, Claude Opus 4.8, Fable 5) against expert ratings on simulated Japanese counseling dialogues, finding that high run-to-run reliability (ICC .33-.96) did not ensure closer alignment with expert judgment. Claude Opus 4.8 showed the smallest mean absolute difference from experts. This highlights a critical gap in LLM-based evaluation: reproducibility does not imply validity. It underscores the need for construct-level validation against professional judgment, especially in high-stakes domains like counseling, where automated evaluation is increasingly used. The study used 18 complete simulated AI-to-AI counseling sessions in Japanese, rated by 15 experts on four motivational interviewing-informed dimensions and overall quality. Each LLM scored each transcript three times; ICCs ranged from .33 to .96, and all LLMs scored higher than experts on softening sustain talk and overall quality.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Intraclass correlation coefficient (ICC) measures how strongly units in the same group resemble each other, commonly used to assess inter-rater reliability. Motivational interviewing (MI) is a collaborative conversational style to strengthen motivation for change, often used in healthcare. LLM evaluators are increasingly used to score dialogue, but their scores may not align with human experts.

## [MDiTFace: Diffusion Transformer with Decoupled Attention for High-Fidelity Mask-Text Facial Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.12631) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces MDiTFace, a customized diffusion transformer framework that uses a unified tokenization strategy to process semantic mask and text inputs, and a novel decoupled attention mechanism to reduce mask-related computational overhead by over 94% while maintaining performance. This work addresses the challenge of effective cross-modal interaction in multimodal facial generation, potentially improving applications in digital content creation, virtual avatars, and human-computer interaction. It also introduces an efficiency technique that could be applied to other diffusion transformer models. The framework uses stacked multivariate transformer blocks that process all conditions synchronously, and the decoupled attention mechanism separates computations into dynamic and static pathways, enabling caching and reuse of static features. Extensive experiments show MDiTFace significantly outperforms competing methods in facial fidelity and conditional consistency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion models are a class of generative models that iteratively denoise random noise to produce data, and transformers are a neural network architecture based on self-attention. In multimodal facial generation, semantic masks provide spatial layout information while text descriptions offer high-level attributes, and effectively combining these modalities is challenging. Traditional feature fusion methods often fail to capture deep cross-modal interactions, leading to suboptimal results.

## [CPGRec+ Balances Accuracy and Diversity in Game Recommendations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.14586) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces CPGRec+, an enhanced framework for personalized video game recommendations, adding two new modules—Preference-informed Edge Reweighting (PER) and Preference-informed Representation Generation (PRG)—to the previous CPGRec system. Experiments on two Steam datasets show CPGRec+ achieves superior accuracy and diversity compared to state-of-the-art models. This work addresses the accuracy-diversity trade-off in game recommender systems, which is crucial for user satisfaction and engagement in the rapidly growing gaming industry. By integrating GNNs with LLMs, it offers a practical approach to improve recommendation quality, potentially influencing future recommender system designs beyond gaming. The PER module assigns signed edge weights to qualitatively distinguish player interests and disinterests, then quantitatively measures preference strength to mitigate over-smoothing in graph convolutions. The PRG module leverages LLMs to generate contextualized descriptions of games and players by reasoning personal preferences from comparing global and personal interests, refining representations. The code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 29, 04:00

**Background**: Graph Neural Networks (GNNs) are commonly used in recommender systems to model user-item interactions, but they often suffer from over-smoothing, where node features become overly similar as network depth increases, hurting performance. Large Language Models (LLMs) have shown potential in enhancing recommendations by providing rich contextual understanding. This paper builds on the earlier CPGRec framework, which aimed to balance accuracy and diversity, and extends it with new modules to better capture preference nuances.

## [Comparative Study of Segmentation Architectures for COVID-19 CT Lesion Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.20459) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper evaluates four deep learning segmentation architectures (Unet, PSPNet, Linknet, FPN) combined with six pre-trained encoders for COVID-19 lesion prediction in CT images, achieving a maximum F1-Score of 98% for binary segmentation and 75-77% for multi-class segmentation across three datasets. This research addresses the lack of standardized performance analysis in medical image segmentation by providing a comprehensive benchmark, which can serve as a reference for future studies and improve diagnostic processes for pandemic diseases. The study integrates four architectures with six encoders (VGG19, DenseNet121, InceptionResNetV2, MobileNetV2, SeresNet101, EfficientNetB0) and conducts both binary and multi-class experiments on three COVID-19 CT datasets. The results highlight the effectiveness of deep learning for precise and efficient segmentation.

**Background**: Image segmentation is a key task in medical imaging, dividing images into meaningful regions to identify structures like tumors or lesions. U-Net, PSPNet, Linknet, and FPN are popular deep learning architectures for this purpose, each with unique mechanisms such as U-Net's encoder-decoder structure and PSPNet's pyramid pooling module for capturing global context.
